2026-09-06 | waypoint-nudge | THE OVERDUE CHECKLIST ROW NOW GETS CHASED, AND THE CHASE TERMINATES. Nothing in this platform reached out when a step the CLIENT owns went past its date — the progress page showed it and that was all. src/nudge/ adds the ladder: text on the due date, email at +2 days, text at +5, and at +9 a task for the CSM with NO client message. There is no fifth rung and there cannot be one — db/migrations/371_waypoint_nudges.sql carries CHECK (step BETWEEN 1 AND 4) and UNIQUE (waypoint_id, step), so the cap is stored rather than remembered by the scheduler. A second partial unique index on (client_id, client_local_date) caps every client at ONE client-facing message per day across ALL their checklist rows, measured in the CLIENT'S own timezone, so three late rows produce one text and not three. Both rows are written BEFORE anything is queued, which is the direct fix for 2026-09-03 when a chase loop sent 51 identical texts to one phone in two hours: the record IS the decision, so sixteen duplicate triggers collapse to one send — proved sequentially AND concurrently. Every exit condition is re-checked at SEND time against the live row, not once at schedule time: done or skipped, deleted or ours now, the paid alternative bought, rung 4 spent, the client replied, STOP or a lawyer, program complete or cancelled. A check that throws returns a STOP, never a neutral answer. No verified contact for a channel is a SKIP not a stop — the rung is spent so an impossible text is not retried forever and the ladder advances. Quiet hours are measured where the CLIENT is, layered on top of the dispatcher's Phoenix window, and a message outside their daytime is not queued at all rather than queued and held. NOTHING SENDS: sendTemplated writes one messages row status='queued' and src/messaging/dispatch.mjs sends it, and a test asserts there is no fetch and no provider import anywhere under src/nudge/. db/migrations/372_regulator_complaints.sql closes the absence src/metro2/letters/catalog.mjs:57-65 named: rounds 4 and 5 are prepared / sent / filed, and filed is unreachable without filed_source='client_reported' — staff cannot set it, no workflow sets it, and raw SQL is refused by a CHECK and a forward-only trigger. The first version of that CHECK used `=` and was therefore decorative: NULL = 'client_reported' is NULL, a CHECK only rejects on FALSE, so state='filed' with no source passed. IS NOT DISTINCT FROM closes it, and the raw-SQL test is what caught it. ON HOLD IS NOT IMPLEMENTED and was not invented — repair_programs.status has no such value and no table in this schema pauses a client; the lever that exists is setting a checklist row to 'blocked'. consent_sms is NOT required, matching every other send path in this repo, and that is recorded as a gap rather than papered over. Copy is placeholder on purpose (db/seed/025) behind three stable keys so Chris swaps the words without touching code. The credit-limit cell holds one of THREE things — a positive number, the number ZERO, or nothing at all — and the code asked only "is it null?". A limit reported as $0 is a KNOWN value, ten percent of it is $0, and that was printed as an instruction: "Pay SECURED CARD from $900 down to $0" in credit_analysis_report.html, funding_snapshot.html and optimization_roadmap.html, and in all four bodies of the WeasyPrint printer, three lines under the same card's own row which correctly printed dashes. Reproduced 2026-09-06 through the real mapper on one open card, SECURED CARD, balance 900, effectiveLimit 0, engine utilization {totalBalance:900,totalLimit:0,pct:null}. A reported $0 and an unreported limit share the OUTCOME — no target — and not the WORDS: telling the holder of a card whose limit IS reported, as $0, that "no credit limit is reported for this card" is its own false sentence. The three printers now say "The credit limit reported for this card is $0" and "No credit limit is reported for this card" respectively, and "No open card on this file reports a credit limit above $0" for the total, which is true in both states. The mapper, the waypoint checklist (src/waypoints/definitions.mjs:235,324) and the vendor engine (vendor/underwriteiq-crs/optimization-findings.js:115) were each opened and already guard on `> 0`; nothing else in the tree runs a reported zero through the 10% arithmetic. You have a clean TransUnion." for every client — rendered against a file with mortgages: [], installments: [] and one AMEX, every clause was false. Each one is now derived from that client's rows or does not appear. (3) A BARE DASH MID-SENTENCE, FOR EVERY CLIENT. `llc_fee` is initialised to null in src/underwrite/black-report-client.mjs:29 and is never assigned anywhere in this repository, so the roadmap read "File your LLC in TX online with the Secretary of State for -." No fee on the file, no fee clause in the sentence. Same shape, same fix, in the pdf-lib printer's "your utilization is -." `score_targets` is initialised to four empty strings and likewise never assigned, so the roadmap's whole month-6 column was four blank cells; all three printers now say "Set at your next pull", which is the pdf-lib printer's own existing wording. (4) THE GUARD NOW COVERS THE THIRD PRINTER, AND THE CLAIM THAT IT ALREADY DID HAS BEEN CORRECTED IN PLACE ABOVE. src/deliverables/three-printer-wording.test.mjs locks the shared sentences across all three at once — the web pages and the pdf-lib printer by importing them, the WeasyPrint printer through a capture that carries the sha256 of fundhub_gen.py so a change that is not recaptured fails rather than drifting. Anything NOT on that list went through. A reviewer ran the real send path with the detail flag off and put "You owe 24000 this month. Pay 900 today." on a lock screen, with the network really called, plus fourteen more — "Pay 900 today.", "Minimum due 95 by Friday.", "Balance 42.5k due.", "Your Prosper loan is past due.", "Bread Financial needs a payment.", "Best Egg statement is ready.", "Your Marcus account was updated.", "Your OppLoans payment is late.", "Your 90-day late was removed.", "Your inquiry removal is done." and the rest. A denylist can never close that — there are thousands of lenders and no end of ways to write a number — so THE RULE IS INVERTED. With the flag off a notification body is not free text at all: it is one of seven complete sentences written into APPROVED_BODIES in that file, chosen by key, none of which holds anything belonging to a client. Anything else is REFUSED, never stripped, and refused permanently rather than retried. Every other string in the payload is whitelisted the same way — the title must be exactly "FundHub", the tap target exactly /app/client-portal.html so identifiers cannot ride in a query string, and the collapse tag one of the six kinds — so the complete set of notifications this system can produce with the flag off is 252 fixed messages, and src/push/payload.test.mjs writes all 252 down. PROVED BY PROPERTY TEST RATHER THAN BY EXAMPLES, because a list of examples is what failed: 20,000 generated bodies (amounts, lender names, credit-repair words, and near-miss copies of the approved sentences carrying trailing spaces, a Greek letter that looks like a u, and zero-width characters) → 17,742 refused, 2,258 allowed, and the allowed ones were exactly the 7 approved sentences and nothing else; 5,000 whole-payload cases across all five fields → 1,184 built, every one inside the 252-payload set; and 250 sends through the real provider with a fake network, DECRYPTING the bytes that fake network was handed rather than trusting the gate ran → 27 arrived and every decrypted body was approved. All fifteen sentences the reviewer got through are now fifteen named tests, each refused. allowDetail still lets free text through when it is on, still defaults to off, and a new test greps the repository and fails if anything outside the push module ever sets it true. AND AN IPHONE ON 16.0-16.3 IS NO LONGER SENT ON AN ERRAND THAT CANNOT WORK: iOS 16.4 is the first version with notifications at all, but the only version warning fired below 16, so a 16.3 phone was shown the three add-to-home-screen steps, did them, and came back to a card that had DISAPPEARED — no message, no reason. The version is now read before anybody is asked to do anything, installed or not, and any iPhone the page cannot serve is told so in one sentence. Measured in a fake browser across ten devices, the page's own code run rather than read (src/push/portal-device-cases.test.mjs): before, 16.0 and 16.3 in Safari got the three steps and 16.3 installed got NO CARD AT ALL; after, 15.7 / 16.0 / 16.3 all get "needs iPhone software version 16.4 or newer" whether installed or not, 16.4 and 17.5 in Safari get the steps, and 17.5 installed, an iPad on iPadOS 17.5, Android 14 and desktop Chrome all get the button. Also fixed there: a guard was added so the version is only read from an iPhone or iPad user agent. To be exact, and correcting this entry's first draft: that guard did NOT fix a live bug. The old pattern required a digit straight after "OS", and a Mac reports "Mac OS X 10_15_7" with an " X " in the way, so it already returned nothing. The guard is belt-and-braces, not a repair. 98/98 push tests pass, run twice. A card is now recognised by its PRINT: the day it was opened plus the last four digits of its number, both already on the credit file and neither rewritten when a creditor string is tidied. When a card carries no print, or the enrolment file held even one card that could not be identified, THE ANSWER IS UNKNOWN AND NOTHING HAPPENS — no flag, no lost paydown, and the reason recorded on the run names the missing fact. It passed locally and in CI only because both connect as a superuser. (7) THE BREACH RECORD ERASED ITSELF AND THE STATE WAS A ONE-WAY DOOR. A re-seed rewrote the no-new-credit baseline from the newest pull, which folded the very account that caused a block into the list of accounts that were always there — the row stayed blocked with a baseline saying nothing had happened, and nothing anywhere moved it back. The baseline is WRITE-ONCE now, and a blocked row whose account is no longer on the file goes back to not_started with its reason cleared. It is never moved to done: keeping a rule is not proof that it was kept. (8) The blocked wording was ungrammatical. It is stored on the row and, as above, no shipped reader renders `state_reason`, so it never reached a client either — "New credit opened after enrolment: CREDIT ONE BANK N.A. account(s)." It now reads "Your credit file now shows an account that was not on it when you enrolled: X. Let your advisor know if this is not yours." — what the file shows, not what the client did, with no amount and no claim about what happens next. MIGRATIONS: 361 and 362 were corrected IN PLACE, which is normally forbidden here and is safe only because neither has ever run against production — they are new on this branch. 363 carries the same two corrections as a conditional UPDATE plus the policy and grant fix, so a database that already applied the earlier versions is repaired too; each copy UPDATE fires only where the text is still exactly what 362 wrote, so a hand-edited row is left alone. MEASURED on a scratch Postgres created and migrated from empty for this run, database fundhub_seedr, 259 migrations: src/waypoints/seed.pg.test.mjs 31/31 pass, exit 0, three times over against the same database. Unit half and database half compared failure-name by failure-name against origin/main on the same machine. NOT PROVED: nothing here was run against the live site, no browser was opened, and no screen lets a client tick a task off. COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED — credit-repair messaging and dispute logic. | A client with no credit pull was being told they qualified for a loan, a bureau's spelling change was accusing clients of opening credit they had not opened, the checklist could never be finished, and the migration that ships it could have failed the deploy. | branch fix/waypoint-seeding-repair
2026-09-06 | waypoint-seeding (new page) | NOTHING HAS EVER CREATED A CLIENT'S CHECKLIST, AND NOW ENROLMENT DOES. `client_waypoints` shipped with migration 330 and `src/waypoints/store.mjs` shipped `upsertWaypoint()` beside it, and on origin/main 8010b1b9 a grep for every caller of that function returned FOUR files — three `*.test.mjs` and one manual check script under `docs/workflows/wave-3-checks/`. NOT ONE ROW HAD EVER BEEN WRITTEN BY THE PRODUCT, so every real client's list was empty, the portal read returned [], and a nudge loop built on the table would have had nothing to chase. Confirmed by running it, not only by reading: enrolling a client on a scratch Postgres with all 255 migrations applied left the table empty. WHAT SEEDS NOW: `src/repair/enroll.mjs` calls `seedClientWaypoints()` beside the `repair.enrolled` emit it already made — the existing enrolment moment, not a second trigger. Eight rows for a client with three cards on file: one paydown per CARD, plus do-not-open-new-credit, take the personal loan now, file the LLC, get the EIN, open a business checking account. THE LIST IS DATA, NOT CODE (migrations 361 and 362). Chris recorded the six-month UnderwriteIQ strategy as NOT FINALISED in TODO.md item 0, so the checklist lives in `waypoint_definitions` and changing what clients are asked to do is an UPDATE, not a release. The application holds SELECT on that table and nothing else — CORRECTED 2026-09-06: that was not true when it was written. 104_app_role.sql's ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES had already granted the app role INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE on every table created after it, so the app held all four the moment 361 created this one; the writes were stopped by row-level security and not by the grant, which meant an UPDATE reported success and changed nothing. Migration 363 REVOKEs the three, and a test reads information_schema.role_table_grants rather than asserting it in prose. WHAT IS DELIBERATELY ABSENT: no DUNS, no Dun & Bradstreet, no net-30 vendor accounts, no Uline/Quill/Grainger, no Paydex — "we dont do DUNS", Chris 2026-09-05, and the platform holds no vendor list, no Paydex field and no business-credit tracking, so such a row could never be closed by anything. A test fails if any of those words reaches the catalog. The dispute letters are not on the client's list either; they are our job. ONE CARD, ONE WAYPOINT — the mistake the code caught. A tri-merge reports the same physical card once per bureau, so the repair simulator profile has EIGHT revolving rows for THREE cards, and the roadmap PDF prints all eight. `mergeByCreditor()` merges them the conservative way: the highest balance any bureau reports against the LOWEST limit any bureau reports, so the target is the strictest one the file can justify and the waypoint only closes when EVERY bureau reporting that card is at or under it. Known and accepted: two different cards from one issuer merge into one waypoint, because a credit file gives us an issuer name and no account number worth keying on — the error is fewer waypoints than cards, never a paydown for a card that does not exist. NEVER DONE ON A GUESS. `verify_kind` (migration 360) names the one machine check that can close a row and NULL means NOTHING THE PLATFORM CAN SEE CLOSES THIS — not "unchecked". The EIN, the business bank account, the LLC filing and the personal loan are all NULL and a credit pull does not touch them. A paydown closes when a re-pull shows the balance at or under the target, stays open above it, and STAYS OPEN when the card is missing from the new file or the file does not report its balance, because unknown is not paid off. `no_new_credit` has evidence in ONE DIRECTION ONLY: a card that was not on the enrolment file is positive evidence the rule was broken and the row goes to `blocked` with the creditor named, and nothing whatever is evidence the rule was KEPT, so that row is never completed automatically. A client who had no credit file at enrolment gets a NULL baseline rather than an empty list, and the check refuses to conclude anything from NULL — [] would later have read every card they have ever owned as newly opened. MONEY IS INTEGER CENTS throughout; `params.target_cents` is an integer and the roadmap's 10%-of-limit rule is the only source of it. Three deadlines are NULL on purpose (get_ein, business_checking, no_new_credit) because the sequence is unsettled and the roadmap contradicts itself on the business steps — a NULL due date is never overdue, which beats inventing a date a real client then gets chased about. EVERY PRICE IS NULL, measured and not assumed: the only priced self-serve product here is a dispute round, which is not the client's job, and we do not file LLCs, get EINs or open bank accounts — the upsell column and its whole path are built and tested, and the day one is priced it is an UPDATE. IDEMPOTENT AND PROVED SO: unique (client_id, key) plus an upsert that does not touch state or completed_at, paydown rows matched to their card by params rather than by key, and an existing due date kept — enrolling the same client twice returns the SAME EIGHT ROW IDS. NOT BUILT AND SAID PLAINLY: `evaluateWaypoints()` has no production caller — the wiring point is the two `INSERT INTO crs_results` in `src/finance/soft-pulls.mjs`, outside this lane's paths — so a re-pull does not move a checklist on its own yet; there is no client-facing tick-box UI; nothing here queues, sends or charges anything. MEASURED: `npm run lint` clean, 1875 files. Unit half 9111 tests / 9108 pass / 0 fail / 3 skipped. Database half on the lane's own scratch Postgres, created and migrated from empty for this run, compared failure-name by failure-name against the same database before these commits. | No client had a checklist, so the whole accountability and upsell layer the portal plan is built on had nothing underneath it. | branch feat/waypoint-seeding-2

The one rule that does hold is the important one — no candidate can be rejected by software, enforced in four independent places (the module, the endpoint, a CHECK, and a trigger). Stamped 16:30 because other sessions were writing hiring code into this same checkout as it was traced — a public apply door, outreach, booking and a bench timer, all listed by path at the top of hiring-actual.md and none of them read or verified here; whoever lands that work owns re-tracing both pages. 2026-09-05 | role-csm | THE CSM IS PAID 10% ON WHAT THEY SELL AND NOTHING ON WHAT THEY COLLECT, AND THE CALLS ARE RENAMED ACCOUNTABILITY CALLS. Owner-set after the Cole Gordon research. db/migrations/346 inserts ONE rule — front_end, role='csm', 10% of cash_collected — and the `collections` basis that 293 opened is DELIBERATELY LEFT WITH NO RULE. That empty basis is the design and not an omission: a rep paid to collect leans on a client in the middle of a call that is supposed to be about how that client is doing, and a rep paid on the next sale is motivated to make this one work first. Role-scoped rather than motion-scoped because 247's CHECK requires a motion rule to also name ONE product, and naming an upsell product list would have meant deciding which of Capital Blueprint, Capital Academy and the rest count — Chris did not say, so it was not guessed; role scope reaches the same place because closers own new business, so every front_end row a CSM holds is an upsell, and per-product motion rules would simply outscore this one later with nothing to undo. Proved end to end: $5,000 collected pays $500, no warnings, and commission_rules holds zero rows on the collections basis. RENAMED: "Post-funding Google Meet interview" and "Mid-journey check-in" are both ACCOUNTABILITY CALLS now. Chris's word and the honest one — the CSM holds the client accountable to what they came here for, records what they say, and mentions what they do not have yet. This supersedes the earlier "AR collections disguised as an interview" framing entirely: naming a call an interview when money and an offer also come up is the part that needs defending later, and naming it what it is does not. The offer is a NUDGE, not a pitch, and a client halfway through one service may be sold another — do not gate the upsell on the current service finishing. A stored decision name is looked up in a closed map, `TIMELINE_WORDS`, and only a value from that map is ever printed; every decision name not on it — including every one invented after the file was written — renders as "progress update". A second layer scrubs the map itself when the module loads: a phrase that asserts a regulator filing is dropped rather than shipped, so adding "cfpb complaint filed" to the list does not publish it. The timeline now names no regulator at all in either direction, because a repair_decision_log row does not carry which round it belongs to and guessing is how a false sentence gets on a screen. WITHDRAWN AS FALSE (2): the entry below says the endpoint answers with facts only and that no string it returns is client-facing copy. None of the three says anything happened, and a test now holds all three by exact value so a FOURTH cannot appear without failing. WITHDRAWN AS FALSE (3): scores.business[].pulledAt was `businesses.updated_at`, a column a database trigger rewrites on EVERY edit to the row — editing a business address repainted the client's score as freshly pulled. It is now always null: `businesses` carries no per-score timestamp in any column and none inside entity_data, and created_at is the insert time, not a pull. Sent renders as sent and never as filed. A rung with no letter row gets NO entry rather than a placeholder. 2026-09-05 | client (paid round) | ROUND 3 — A SHORT PAYMENT IS NO LONGER A PAYMENT, AND TWO SENTENCES IN THE LAST REPORT WERE WRONG. THE DEFECT, MEASURED NOT THEORISED: nothing compared the amount a payment webhook REPORTED against the amount the client was BILLED. Driven against scratch Postgres `fundhub_paidr` with the real functions: `recordPayment(amountCents: 0)` on a 10000-cent round returned `applied:true` and the row read `status='paid'`; a webhook of `amountCents: 1` against an 11000-cent round returned `done:true` with reason "round staged, not mailed", the row at `staged`, and a REAL `soft_pull_requests` row queued — a fresh credit report ordered and the round put on a human's board, bought for one cent. WHAT CHANGED: `recordPayment()` in `src/paid-services/round.mjs` now compares the two figures and refuses a short one with its own code, `payment_short` (new in `src/paid-services/refusals.mjs`, its own client-safe sentence, HTTP 409). A new `closeShortPaid()` closes the request `failed` with `state_reason='payment_short: received N of M cents, short by K'`, and KEEPS `paid_at`, `amount_paid_cents` and `payment_ref` on the row so the sum that DID arrive is not lost to whoever refunds or chases it — 331's `paid_service_requests_paid_ck` binds the timestamp and the amount together, so recording one requires the other, and `paid_state_ck` does not forbid either on `failed`. `src/handlers/paid-service-payment.mjs` logs the shortfall at the same volume as the pull failure and returns `{done:false, reason:'payment_short', mailed:false}` without staging. THREE THINGS ARE DELIBERATELY NOT REFUSED, EACH WITH ITS OWN TEST: an UNKNOWN amount (null) still falls back to the quote and records `payment_amount_source='quote'` — NULL MEANS UNKNOWN and unknown must not become an accusation; an OVERpayment runs the work, because the client is not short; and a row whose `price_total_cents` is NULL has nothing to compare against. The comparison is strictly `<`, so an EXACT payment is accepted — stated in its own test because every other test here pays exactly and a wrong comparator would refuse the ordinary case. A KNOWN GAP, WRITTEN DOWN RATHER THAN ROUNDED OFF: a NEGATIVE stated amount is NOT caught. `known` requires `>= 0`, so `amountCents: -50` falls through to the quote fallback and is recorded as a full payment. 331's `paid_service_requests_paid_amount_ck` refuses to store a negative, so there is nowhere to put the figure, and a completed charge does not arrive negative — a refund is its own event. Left as it is, and said here so nobody reads the guard as total. VERIFIED BY EXECUTION, not by reading a test name — probe against `fundhub_paidr`, real functions, only the processor faked: prices 10000 / 11000 / 12000 / 13000 cents, all `Number.isInteger` true, add-ons derived at 1000 and 2000; two CONCURRENT presses gave 1 row and 1 mint call, answers `created:true` and `already_in_flight`; payment landing gave `status=staged`, `produced.mailed=false`, `dispute_letters` 0 rows, `soft_pull_requests` 1 row, and `repair_programs.rounds_cap` still 6; and each refusal returned its own reason — `payment_short` 409 (row `failed`, 0 pulls), `already_in_flight` 409, `payment_failed` 502 (row `failed`), `nothing_to_dispute` 409, `not_on_offer_path` 403. `pull_failed` is proved in the pg suite's consent-revoked case, not re-driven in the probe. MIGRATION 345's CLEANUP WAS NARROWER THAN ITS OWN COMMENT CLAIMED, AND IS FIXED. The comment said the index refuses to build only when two rows carry money. The UPDATE above it cancelled a duplicate only when some OTHER row was strictly OLDER, so one older `quoted` row beside one newer `paid` row cancelled NOTHING and the build failed — on production that is a failed deploy in a state the last report described as safe. REPRODUCED EXACTLY (index dropped, one `quoted` at now()-2 days, one `paid` at now()-1 day, migration re-run): `UPDATE 0` then `ERROR: could not create unique index "uq_paid_service_requests_one_open" ... is duplicated`. Step 1 is now keyed on what the rows ARE rather than which arrived first — any unpaid row yields to a paid sibling in EITHER direction, and unpaid siblings still settle oldest-first as 090 did. RE-MEASURED ON THE SAME STATE: `UPDATE 1`, `CREATE INDEX`, the paid row untouched, the quoted row `cancelled` with its reason, two rows still present — NOTHING DELETED. THE INTENDED REFUSAL IS PRESERVED, checked on purpose: two `paid` rows for one client and kind still give `UPDATE 0` and still refuse the build, because that is a real double charge and a migration must not tidy it away. Three unpaid duplicates still keep the oldest and cancel two. Editing 345 rather than superseding it is correct here and only here: it is NOT on `origin/main`, so it has never run on production — but a scratch database that already applied the OLD 345 will not re-run the new one (CLAUDE.md §12), so re-create scratch databases rather than reusing them. TWO WORDINGS CORRECTED, NO CODE BEHIND THEM: the new database rule is one open paid request per person PER SERVICE KIND, not per person — verified against the live index `(client_id, service_kind)` and 331's `kind_ck`, which allows three kinds, so one person can hold three open paid requests at once; and "a real payment does not reach this yet" now belongs in the description of what was built, not only in the not-built list. STILL NOT DONE, SAID PLAINLY: the handler is STILL NOT ON THE LIVE BUS. `src/register-all.mjs` does not import `src/handlers/paid-service-payment.mjs` — that file is not this lane's — so `payment.received` does not reach `onPaidServicePaymentReceived`, a real paid round would sit at `awaiting_payment`, and EVERY payment behaviour described above is proved by driving the handler and the module directly, NOT by a payment arriving on its own. Checked rather than assumed: the other four `payment.received` handlers ARE registered, through aliased module imports in `register-all.mjs`; this one is the only unregistered one of the five. `docs/diagrams/event-flow.md` lists it beside those four with nothing marking it inert; that page is generated, so it cannot be corrected by hand here. Nothing moves a request from `staged` to `fulfilled`. Checked against the table a mailing would have to touch — `dispute_letters` row count does not move and no row reaches `status='sent'` — and `produced.mailed` on the request reads `false`. The handler imports no mail function. NO SILENT CARD CAPTURE: the only rail is a hosted checkout link (`src/payments/commas-api.mjs`), the session carries `link_ref: <request id>` so the webhook finds its own request, and there is no amount matching — a client's unrelated payment cannot be attached to a round. The processor-facing title is "Document round N" because `commas-safe-copy.mjs` bans credit and funding words from outbound copy; that is asserted by a test, not remembered. A DEFECT FOUND AND CLOSED, MEASURED NOT THEORISED: 331's two unique indexes were NOT enough to make a double press one row. Two presses milliseconds apart both read "nothing open", then one read `MAX(round_no)` before the other's INSERT and the other read it after — different slots, different derived idempotency keys, TWO rows and TWO checkout pages for one press of one button. Repeatable against a real Postgres. `db/migrations/345_paid_service_one_open.sql` adds `uq_paid_service_requests_one_open` (one open request per client per service kind), the identical fix 090 made for `soft_pull_requests`. Ten simultaneous presses now produce ONE row and ONE minted link, at the module and over HTTP, both tested. THE EXCEPTION, WRITTEN DOWN RATHER THAN ROUNDED OFF: a losing press gets one of two answers depending on which microsecond it lost in — the winner's row with `created:false`, or a 409 already_in_flight. Both are correct and both are one row and one charge; the code does not promise which. REFUSALS, EACH WITH ITS OWN TEST AND ITS OWN EFFECT ON THE ROW: not on an offer path 403 (no row); already in flight 409 (no second row, no second link); nothing to dispute 409 (no row); payment failed 502 with the row closed as `failed` so a processor outage does not lock the client out of retrying, and nothing charged; the fresh report refused after payment 502 with the row `failed`, `state_reason=pull_refused:…`, and `paid_at`/`amount_paid_cents` KEPT, because money was taken and a human has to see it. UNKNOWN SURVIVES: a client never pulled, a report retention has tombstoned, and a demo-only report all read as UNKNOWN and are ALLOWED — never having been pulled is not grounds to refuse. Only a report we can actually read with no negatives refuses. A webhook with no amount falls back to the quote and records `produced.payment_amount_source="quote"` so a quoted figure is never read as a confirmed one. WITHDRAWN AS FALSE: the entry below this one, the journey page and the commit message all said "a funding pack carries five analysis documents, not four" and reported F46 closed. THAT SENTENCE IS ONLY TRUE OF AN ORDINARY CLIENT. THE TRUTH IS FIVE FOR AN ORDINARY CLIENT AND SIX FOR A THIN-FILE OR AUTHORIZED-USER-DOMINANT CLIENT. `vendor/underwriteiq-full/api/lite/crs/build-documents.js:162-168` pushes a sixth spec, `business_prep_summary`, whenever `consumerSignals.tradelines.thinFile` is true or `consumerSignals.tradelines.auDominance` is over 0.6; `src/underwrite/letter-pack.mjs:29` lets it into the funding pack and `:87` names it `Business-Readiness-Guide.pdf`; `summary-doc-generator.js:433` renders it and titles it "Business Readiness Guide". The saver dropped it at the identical line the fifth was dropped at — `if (!isLetter && !analysisType) continue;` in `src/underwrite/funding-letter-pdf.mjs` — with no log and no counter. WHAT CHANGED: (1) `FUNDING_ANALYSIS_SUBTYPE` now carries a sixth key, `business_prep_summary` → subtype `business_prep_summary`, titled "Business Readiness Guide" — the vendor's own type name and its own renderer's own title, neither invented here; the filename fallback recognises both `business_prep_summary.pdf` and `Business-Readiness-Guide.pdf`. No migration: `documents.subtype` is not constrained by the database. (2) THE ROOT CAUSE WAS THE SILENCE, NOT THE MISSING KEYS, so the silence is what is fixed. Every file handed to `persistFundingLetterFiles` now lands in exactly one of three buckets and they always add up to the number of files in: `stored`; `notStored` with a stated reason (a Metro 2 dispute letter, a CFPB or state AG complaint and its cover sheet, a repair-pack summary, staff-only paperwork, a funding letter with no bureau, or a file that arrived with no bytes); or `unrecognised`, which is counted, named, and written to the log as `[funding-letter-pdf] N file(s) NOT SAVED`. Passing `strict: true` makes it throw instead. A seventh document added to the pack tomorrow cannot vanish the way these two did. MEASURED BY RUNNING IT, not asserted — scratch Postgres `fundhub_fixr1`, 239 migrations applied to an empty database, the REAL `buildLetterPackForClient` and the REAL `persistFundingLetterFiles`, both clients carrying the repo's own `academy` simulated credit file (tiers FULL_FUNDING); the authorized-user-dominant client is that same file with nine of its twelve tradelines flipped to `accountOwnershipType: "AuthorizedUser"`, giving `auDominance` 0.75: an ORDINARY client's pack carried 5 analysis files and stored 5 rows (`bank_lender_match_list`, `credit_analysis_report`, `credit_optimization_roadmap`, `funding_snapshot`, `funding_summary`); an AU-DOMINANT client's pack carried 6 and stored 5 BEFORE this change and 6 after (the same five plus `business_prep_summary`, titled Business Readiness Guide, with real bytes behind it); `unrecognised` was empty for both. Handed a made-up `lender_pitch_deck` file the saver stored the rest, logged `[funding-letter-pdf] 1 file(s) NOT SAVED — the saver does not recognise them: Lender-Pitch-Deck.pdf (type lender_pitch_deck)` and returned it in `unrecognised`; with `strict: true` the same call threw. NEGATIVE CONTROL RUN: with the sixth subtype, its title and its filename rule removed, 8 of the 27 unit tests fail. (3) ALSO CORRECTED — THE HTML GUARD DIAGRAM WAS WRONG ABOUT THE 302 BRANCH. The journey page drew `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox; default-src 'none'` and `Content-Disposition: attachment` as covering both storage paths. They do not cover the redirect: headers on a 302 describe the redirect, and the browser then makes a separate request to the blob host which carries none of them, so an HTML document served that way would still render on the blob host. 2026-09-05 | repair-letter-send | CORRECTION 2, AND THE FIX FOR IT: A LETTER THE MAIL COMPANY HAD ALREADY TAKEN COULD BE SENT A SECOND TIME. WHAT I TOLD YOU BEFORE THAT WAS WRONG: the two entries below say "mailing the same dispute letter twice is now impossible". That word was false, and I am not going to use it here. THE DEFECT: the whole guard rests on writing down "this letter was mailed" the moment the mail company takes it. The code only wrote that down when the mail company also handed back an ID number for the letter. The mail company can accept a letter and answer with no ID. When that happened the envelope went out and our records said nothing had. The letter then showed up on the staff list of "stuck" letters, a staff member could release it, and it went out again — a second envelope to a real person and a second bill. The same thing happened if the database hiccuped for a moment during that one write, because that write's error was thrown away without a word to anyone. MEASURED ON A REAL DATABASE ON 2026-09-05, before the fix: press one, the mail company said yes with no ID, one envelope, our row said "not mailed"; the row was then offered to staff as stuck; the release worked; press two, a second envelope. WHAT THE CODE DOES NOW. (1) THE FACT THAT MATTERS IS THAT THE MAIL COMPANY TOOK IT, not that it gave us an ID. The mailing is written down on a yes, ID or no ID. The ID column stays empty in that case, and empty means UNKNOWN, never "this did not happen". (2) THAT ONE WRITE IS NO LONGER SILENT. It is tried twice, and if it still cannot be written the letter's result says so, the whole send carries a list called `unrecordedMailings`, and a line goes to the log that says DO NOT RE-SEND IT. (3) THE DATABASE ITSELF NOW REFUSES TO UN-MAIL A LETTER. `db/migrations/334` adds two rules Postgres enforces on every write from now on, so a hand-typed database command is refused exactly as the app is. (4) THE LIVE SEND BUTTON now passes the mail company's own answer to "did anything actually leave this building?" through to the guard, instead of throwing it away and guessing from the wording of the error. WHAT IS STILL TRUE AND NOT FIXED, both of them stated plainly because the last two versions of this note left them out: a letter sent with no saved row has nothing to write on, so nothing stops a second one — that is unchanged behaviour and the app has always allowed an ad-hoc letter; and if the database cannot be reached for BOTH tries of that one write, our records still do not know the envelope went, so the database cannot refuse a release of it. That case is now loud instead of silent, but loud is not the same as prevented. PROVED BY RUNNING IT on a scratch database: a yes with no ID records the mailing and the letter can no longer be released or re-sent (one mail company call, not two); a database error on that write is reported instead of swallowed; a refusal that provably never reached the network still gives the letter back; a genuinely stuck letter still clears and can then be sent; and two presses at the same instant still produce exactly one envelope. | branch feat/portal-schema

2026-09-05 | repair-letter-send | CORRECTION, AND THE FIX FOR IT: THE GUARD THAT STOPS A DISPUTE LETTER BEING MAILED TWICE WAS DESTROYING LETTERS THAT WERE NEVER MAILED AT ALL. The entry below this one told Chris that when a send failed, "the row stays claimed for a person to reconcile". BOTH HALVES OF THAT SENTENCE WERE UNTRUE OF THE CODE, and the second one hid the worse half of the problem. (1) IT WAS NOT ONLY A FAILED SEND. The guard marked a letter "mailed" the moment it picked the letter up, BEFORE it called the mail company at all. So a send that the safety fence held — where nothing was transmitted and the network was provably never touched — still left the letter marked as mailed and unable to ever be sent. (2) THERE WAS NO PERSON WHO COULD RECONCILE IT, AND THE DAMAGE WAS NOT LIMITED TO THAT ONE LETTER. A brand new replacement letter for the same person, same credit bureau, same round and same destination was refused as well, because the guard counted the false mark. Nothing anywhere in this system could clear either one. The letter and every future replacement for it were dead. MEASURED ON A REAL DATABASE ON 2026-09-05, three presses, with a network call rigged to throw an error if anything ever reached it — it never fired, so nothing left the building: press one, the fence held the call and the row was marked sending with a mailing time stamped on it; press two on the same letter with the fence off was refused "already mailed" and the mail company was called zero times; press three on a brand new replacement row was refused "already mailed duplicate letter", also zero calls. A GENUINE MAIL-COMPANY OUTAGE BRICKED THE LETTER THE SAME WAY. WHAT THE CODE DOES NOW, and this is a literal description of it. THE ONE FACT IS NOW CARRIED, NOT GUESSED FROM WORDING: `src/lib/outbound-fetch.mjs`, the single place this system makes an outbound call, returns `transmitted` on every result, and every `false` is returned from a branch that sits ABOVE the call, so it is proved by which line ran and not by reading an error message. A timeout and a dropped connection both report `transmitted: true`, because the vendor may have accepted the work before the connection died. `src/messaging/providers/mail-letter.mjs` carries that up as `preTransmission` on every failure it returns, and `src/repair/send.mjs` reads that fact BEFORE it reads any message text. It used to read only the text, against a list of eight fixed phrases, and the fence's wording was not one of them. THE PHRASE LIST STILL EXISTS AND THAT IS DELIBERATE: two callers rebuild the mail result and drop the fact — the closure in `api/repair/send.mjs` and the address refusals in `src/metro2/delivery/send.mjs` — and every one of those is genuinely a refusal before transmission, so the list is what still catches them. Delete the list and they start holding letters they should release; trust the list alone and this defect comes straight back. TWO FACTS NOW LIVE IN TWO COLUMNS (`db/migrations/333_dispute_letter_send_claim.sql`): `send_claimed_at` means "this letter is taken", is stamped before the call, and RELEASES; `mailed_at` means "this letter was mailed", is stamped only after the mail company answered with an identifier, and is NEVER released by anything or anybody. There is one unique index on each. The mailing index is the hard promise — one physical mailing per case, bureau, round and destination, for ever, and nothing in the code can undo a row's membership of it. The claim index is a deliberate superset of it, which is what lets a regenerated replacement for a letter that really did go out be refused BEFORE the call rather than after an envelope is already in the post. THE STUCK STATE NOW HAS A WAY OUT: `clearStuckSendClaim()` in `src/repair/send.mjs` lets a member of staff who has checked the mail company's own records say "this one did not go", with their identifier and their written reason, both stored on the row and written to the decision log that `src/repair/lens.mjs` renders in plain English on the client's timeline. It refuses unless the row is claimed, carries no mailing, carries no mail-company identifier, and the claim is at least fifteen minutes old — the network call's own hard cut-off is ten seconds, so it cannot race a call that is merely slow. IT CLEARS THE CLAIM AND NEVER THE MAILING, so it cannot turn a letter that really went out into one that may go out again. IT CAN BE WRONG THE OTHER WAY, and that is accepted and stated: if the letter did reach the mail company and only the reply was lost, clearing lets a second one go out — which is exactly why it is a deliberate, named, recorded human act and why nothing calls it on a timer. RE-STAGING WAS NOT A WAY OUT EITHER, AND IS NOW HONEST: `src/repair/analyze.mjs` did not count a letter sitting in `sending` or `delivered` as already staged, so re-staging a round wrote a SECOND letter row, which the index then refused at send time — another dead letter, not a recovery. Both states are now counted, so a re-stage reports the round as already staged and hands back the existing letter. A DEFECT IN THE REPAIR ITSELF, FOUND BY RUNNING IT AND FIXED: the new migration originally cleared the false mailing marks while the OLD rule that required them was still in force, so `node db/migrate.mjs` against a scratch database carrying one stuck row aborted with a constraint violation and applied nothing — meaning the fix could not have reached any database that had actually been used, which is the only kind production is. The old rule is now dropped first. `src/repair/send-claim-migration-order.test.mjs` fails if that order is ever reversed. PROVED BY RUNNING IT, EVERY LINE WATCHED: on a scratch Postgres, a send the fence held releases the letter and the very next press really mails it; a genuine mail-company error keeps the claim and a retry calls nobody; a replacement letter claims cleanly after a released one; two simultaneous presses on the same letter call the mail company EXACTLY ONCE and two simultaneous presses on two duplicate rows also mail once; a stuck claim is cleared by a named person and the letter is sendable again; clearing is refused on a letter that really was mailed; and the database itself refuses to release a mailing. Separately, a scratch database was put back into the OLD shape with three real-world rows in it — one bricked, one genuinely mailed, one predating the guard — and the new migration was applied to it: the bricked row's false mailing mark was cleared and its claim preserved, the genuine mailing was left exactly as it was, the pre-guard row's unknown stayed unknown as NULL, and the bricked row was then cleared by a named person end to end. MEASURED, NOT ASSERTED: `npm run lint` clean, 1800 files. Unit half 8752 tests / 8749 pass / 0 fail / 3 skipped. Database half on a scratch Postgres created and migrated from empty for this run: 2309 tests / 2293 pass / 15 fail / 1 skip; `origin/main` at 2d81f1c5 run the identical way on its own freshly created scratch database gives 2278 / 2261 / 16 / 1, and comparing the failing test NAMES with the timings stripped there are ZERO new failures — all 15 appear on main too, and main carries one more that passes here. NOT BUILT, and said plainly: there is NO SCREEN AND NO WEB ADDRESS for clearing a stuck letter. `clearStuckSendClaim()` and `listStuckSendClaims()` are written, exported and proved, and a member of staff cannot reach either without a developer, because the files that add a web address are another lane's. The exact wiring is written into the task report. Nothing writes `mail_cost_cents` yet. | A safety guard meant to stop a person receiving two identical dispute letters was instead permanently destroying letters that were never sent, including every replacement for them, with no way for anyone to undo it. | branch feat/portal-schema

No route, screen, tab or menu row was added — deliberately, and that omission is named above rather than glossed.
2026-09-05 | repair-letter-send (new page) | [SUPERSEDED — the "IMPOSSIBLE" in this headline was false when it was written; the 2026-09-05 entry at the top of this file says what is and is not true now] PRESSING SEND TWICE ON A DISPUTE LETTER THAT HAS A SAVED ROW MAILS IT ONCE, AND THE TWO TABLES THE CLIENT PORTAL SITS ON EXIST. THE DEFECT: `src/repair/send.mjs` set a letter to `sent` AFTER the mail provider answered, with no check of what the status already was, and `dispute_letters` carried no unique index of any kind. So posting the same send payload a second time handed the same letters to PostGrid again — two identical envelopes to the consumer, two to the credit bureau, and two bills. The only thing in the way was a disabled button in a browser, which a retry, a second tab or a command-line request walks straight past. THE FIX, IN THE DATABASE AND NOT IN THE BROWSER: `db/migrations/332_dispute_letter_mail_guard.sql` adds `mailed_at`, a `sending` status, and `uq_dispute_letters_one_mailing`, a unique index over (org, case, bureau, round, destination) covering every row that carries a `mailed_at`. `src/repair/send.mjs` now CLAIMS the letter in one statement BEFORE the provider is called, so two callers cannot both pass the check — the second one's update matches nothing and it never makes the call; a duplicate letter row for the same case, bureau, round and destination is refused by the index instead of becoming a second envelope. THE INDEX KEYS ON `mailed_at` AND NOT ON `status` ON PURPOSE: `mailed_at` is a new column, every existing row is NULL, NULL is outside the index and is never backfilled, so the guard is total from this migration forward and not one historic row of real mailings had to be rewritten to make the index build. A CLAIM THAT MIGHT HAVE TRANSMITTED IS NEVER AUTO-RETRIED — if the provider call was made and did not come back we do not know whether the letter went, so the row stays claimed for a person to reconcile; the claim is released ONLY for the fixed set of refusals that happen above the network call (no API key, no return address, no destination, no PDF or HTML, private carrier to a PO box). REFUSE ONLY ON POSITIVE EVIDENCE: a letter id naming no row is still mailed, exactly as it was before, so the guard cannot start silently dropping sends it has no evidence against. ALSO IN THIS COMMIT, the schema the portal needs: `client_waypoints` (330) is the per-client ordered checklist — whose job each step is, its state, `completed_at`, `due_at` so "overdue" is a computed fact and never a guess, and `paid_alternative_price_cents`, the column that makes it an upsell system rather than a to-do list, present from the first migration. It is a NEW table and deliberately not `tasks`: `tasks_assignee_role_ck` (041) allows staff roles only and `src/lib/create-task.mjs:70` throws because "Principal types (client, affiliate, partner) must never own internal work". `paid_service_requests` (331) is one row per "do it for me", general enough for a dispute round today and a credit pull or funding application later, storing the priced LINE ITEMS rather than a bare number — owner-set $100 flat per round covering all three bureaus, +$10 for a creditor letter, +$20 for the CFPB and state attorney general filings — with a CHECK that the lines add up to the total, and a partial unique index on (org, idempotency key) so a double press is one row, the same index `events` and `soft_pull_requests` already use. NULL SURVIVES EVERYWHERE AND ZERO IS REFUSED: a waypoint with no paid alternative stores NULL and the database refuses 0 outright, so nothing can read a zero and call it free; a due date nobody set is NULL and is never overdue; `dispute_letters.mail_cost_cents` starts NULL meaning unknown, not free. A PAID ROUND DOES NOT CONSUME A PURCHASED ROUND (owner-set): `round_no` on the new table is the self-serve counter, `repair_programs.rounds_cap` is the program counter, and there is deliberately no column joining them — a test asserts the cap does not move and that no such column exists. MEASURED, NOT ASSERTED: `npm run lint` clean, 1797 files. Unit half 8728 tests / 8725 pass / 0 fail / 3 skipped. Database half on a scratch Postgres created and migrated from empty for this run: 2300 tests / 2284 pass / 15 fail / 1 skip, and `origin/main` run the identical way on its own freshly created scratch database gives 2278 / 2262 / 15 / 1 with a BYTE-IDENTICAL list of the same 15 failing test names — no new failure. The double-mailing claim is proved by running it: the same payload posted twice calls the mailer once and leaves one letter row carrying the FIRST send's provider id; a duplicate row for the same case, bureau, round and destination is refused and keeps a NULL `mailed_at`; a pre-transmission refusal gives the letter back and it then sends; an HTTP error keeps the claim and a retry calls nothing. NOT BUILT, and said plainly: nothing writes `mail_cost_cents` yet — reading a price off the provider response is a later change, so the margin on the $100 is recordable but not yet recorded; no endpoint reads either new table and no screen exists; and a letter posted with no letter id still has no row to claim, which is unchanged behaviour and not a new hole. | Re-posting a send mailed a real person's dispute letters twice and billed twice, and this had to be closed before any client-facing "buy another round" button exists. | branch feat/portal-schema

2026-09-04 | repair-documents | CORRECTION, AND THE FIX FOR IT: THE PHOTO CLASSIFIER USED TO CALL THINGS A GOVERNMENT ID THAT THE DOCUMENT AGENT HAD JUST SAID WERE NOT ONE. The entry below this one told Chris that an unclear answer "falls back to other rather than guessing". THAT SENTENCE WAS NOT TRUE OF THE CODE. The word-match list in `src/handlers/inbound-mms-docs.mjs` ended with a bare `\bid\b`, which matches the word "id" in any sentence — including every sentence saying there is no ID in the picture. Run against the real classifier, all five of these were filed as `id_document`: "No valid ID was visible in this photo", "not an ID", "the photo is too blurry to read the ID", "a selfie, no id card", and "a driver license belonging to someone other than the client". WHY THAT IS SERIOUS: `id_document` is one leg of the identity gate in `src/inquiry-ops/doc-gate.mjs`, and that gate is what decides whether dispute letters may be posted to the credit bureaus in a client's name. A picture of nothing satisfied it, and `src/repair/handlers.mjs` then moved the client's card on to `analysis` as though their identity were proved. WHAT THE CODE DOES NOW, and this is a literal description of it: the bare `\bid\b` match is gone; a phrase is refused if it carries any denial or doubt word (no, not, cannot, unable, missing, unreadable, illegible, unclear, unknown, unsure, invalid, expired, blurry, obscured, glare, maybe, might, possibly, probably, appears, seems, looks, likely, either, or, another, someone, else, third-party, mismatch, and the rest of the list at `NEGATION_OR_HEDGE`); a phrase longer than FOUR words is refused (six let "driver license of the client's brother" and "handwritten note saying ID card" through as a government ID; four refuses both and still clears "Arizona driver license" and "state issued photo id"), and a phrase naming another person or describing the picture rather than the document is refused, because the agent is asked for the plainest NAME of the document and anything longer is a sentence about the photo; a reply whose `outcome` is anything but `accept`, or which carries a `hold_reason` or any listed issue, is refused outright, so a document the agent did not accept is never typed as a verified identity document; when the agent returns more than one phrase they must all agree on one type, and a single unreadable phrase spoils the whole reply; anything else stays `other`, exactly as it was before this batch. THE FILENAME, THE SENDER AND THE ORDER THE PICTURES ARRIVED IN ARE STILL NEVER READ. A photo that stays `other` is now recorded with `classified_by: null` instead of the agent's name, so a picture nobody could read never looks agent-verified. PROVED BY RUNNING IT, not asserted: the exported `classifyMmsImage()` was driven with each of the five sentences above and the real `checkDocPacket()` was then asked what it sees — all five now file as `other` with `docGateSeesId:false`; "Arizona driver license" files as `id_document` with `docGateSeesId:true`; "possibly a passport" files as `other` with `docGateSeesId:false`; and a `driver's license` the agent put on hold files as `other` with `docGateSeesId:false`. ALSO IN THIS COMMIT: the handler's import of the document agent was still pointing at the old `./ghl-doc.mjs`, which the GoHighLevel rename deleted — every inbound picture message would have crashed on load. It now imports `./doc-check.mjs`. THREE THINGS THIS DOES NOT FIX, written into the journey page so nobody reads it as finished: (a) WITHDRAWN AS FALSE, 2026-09-04 — an earlier draft of this entry said the document agent row carries no instructions on a freshly migrated database. It does: a virgin database with all 239 migrations applied to empty returns `DOC-CHECK | live | prompt length 3275` and classification works. The wrong figure came from querying a database the pg test half had already mutated, where the same query returns `DOC-CHECK | draft | 0`. The corrected measurement is on `docs/journeys/repair-documents-actual.md`; (b) the credit-repair contract check `canLeaveIntake()` still does not run on the enrolment path (COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED); (c) nothing moves a card out of `analysis` without a member of staff pressing the button. | The sentence Chris was shown described a refusal the code did not perform, and the wrong label landed on the gate that mails letters to credit bureaus. | pending
2026-09-04 | repair-documents (new page) | THE REPAIR DOCUMENT PATH IS CONNECTED END TO END FOR THE FIRST TIME. Three separate breaks, all in the same run of steps, none of them a missing feature — every piece already existed and none of them was wired to the next. (1) A REPAIR CLIENT COULD NOT SEE THE DOOR THEY UPLOAD THEIR ID THROUGH. `src/repair/upload-doors.mjs` opened the door carrying `id_document` and `proof_of_address` on the `funding-snapshot` entitlement alone; a repair client is granted `metro2-letter-pack`, so the only door they ever saw was "Upload your bureau response". The identity documents are the first thing the program needs and there was nowhere to send them. `lanesFromEntitlementCodes()` now returns `hasIdentityLane` = funding OR repair and `activeUploadDoors()` reads it; funding clients are unchanged; `public/app/client-portal.html`'s own copy of that rule is changed to match, and the door's label goes from "Funding documents" to "ID and personal documents" because it now serves both lanes. NO NEW DOOR, SCREEN OR TAB — the same door on the same card. (2) THE "WE NEED YOUR DOCUMENTS" STAGE HAD NEVER BEEN REACHED BY ANYONE. `awaiting_documents` is in `src/repair/pipeline.mjs`, the client sentence "Upload your ID and proof of address to continue" is in `src/repair/portal.mjs`, the 14-day chase is in `src/repair/sla.mjs`, and `src/repair/handlers.mjs` already branched on `repair.docs.needed` and `repair.docs.complete` — AND NOTHING IN THE REPOSITORY EMITTED EITHER EVENT, so every repair client since the rail was written sat on `intake` and was never asked for anything. `announceRepairDocState()` in `src/repair/handlers.mjs` is the missing emitter: it fires on `repair.enrolled`, and `onRepairDocsReceived` (registered on `docs.received` in `src/repair/register.mjs`) fires when an upload lands. THE "HAVE THEY ARRIVED" QUESTION IS NOT ANSWERED TWICE: `checkDocPacket()` in `src/inquiry-ops/doc-gate.mjs` is imported, not copied. It reads that check's `present` flags rather than its `complete` flag ON PURPOSE — `complete` also requires the signed authorization, which is its own gate on its own timeline, and this stage is only about the two images the document agent reads. (3) A TEXTED PHOTO WAS FILED AS "other". `src/handlers/inbound-mms-docs.mjs` registered every inbound picture with subtype `other`, so neither the document agent nor the document gate could tell a driver's licence from a gas bill from a photograph of a dog — a client who texted their ID had, to every check downstream, sent nothing. The image is now shown to DOC-CHECK (the same seeded agent, renamed from GHL-DOC by migration 310 that already reads these pictures, `db/migrations/114_ghl_agent_seed.sql`) and its own `documents_reviewed` answer is mapped onto the `client_upload` subtypes BEFORE the document is filed, so `docs.received` carries the right type. THE FILENAME IS NEVER READ — an MMS filename is `mms-<message id>-<n>` and carries nothing. THREE REFUSALS, EACH TESTED: a documents table that will not answer emits nothing at all (unknown stays unknown; a client is never told they have not sent something on the strength of a failed read); an upload only acts on a card sitting on `intake` or `awaiting_documents`, so a round-five client texting a bureau letter is never dragged back to `analysis`; and a photo is only typed as a government ID when the agent plainly named one (see the 2026-09-04 correction at the top of this file for exactly what "plainly" means). PROVED ON A REAL POSTGRES, not asserted: a $200 trial client enrolled on a scratch database gets `rounds_cap` 2 (owner-set, unchanged), holds only `metro2-letter-pack`, and their doors read `{funding:true, inquiry:false, bureau_response:true}` — the identity door open; `repair.enrolled` then `repair.docs.needed` are both on the events table and the card sits on `awaiting_documents`; landing the ID alone leaves it there; landing the proof of address emits `repair.docs.complete` and the card moves to `analysis`; a texted photo whose agent answer is "Arizona driver license" is written to the documents table as `subtype=id_document`, stamped as the document agent's decision. Unit half 8669 tests / 8666 pass / 0 fail / 3 skipped. Database half 2272 tests / 15 failures, byte-identical failure names to `origin/main` measured the same hour on its own scratch database — no new failure. `npm run lint` clean. | Three breaks in one line of steps meant no repair client had ever been asked for the ID and proof of address the whole program is built on. | branch feat/repair-doc-path

2026-09-04 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | LENDER MATCHING NOW READS BOTH OF A CLIENT'S STATES, NOT ONE. Owner rule: "It's based off of home state and home address, which is their personal address, and then also their business address as well. So if they live in Arizona but they also have a business in Florida, that opens up two lanes of opportunity to national banks and then local banks that only do business in those two states." `resolveMatchState()` in src/lenders/match.mjs returned ONE state and returned it the moment it found a business row, so the home state was a fallback that never ran once a business existed. An Arizona client with a Florida business was matched as Florida only, and every Arizona-only local bank was dropped without even appearing in `skipped` — the state gate never reached it. REPLACED BY `resolveMatchStates()`, which returns `{ home, business, states[] }`, plus `resolveHomeState()` and `resolveBusinessState()` so the two questions are asked separately instead of down one blended chain. The gate is now `eligibleForAnyState()`: a lender is eligible when it covers EITHER state. `stateEligible()` is UNCHANGED and still treats an empty or "all states" footprint as national. UNKNOWN STILL BLOCKS NOBODY, in both directions: an empty state set passes every lender exactly as an empty single state did, and a lender with a blank `eligible_states` is still included and is now LABELLED `footprint: "unknown"` rather than being called national, so a blank cell is not read as a promise. THE RESULT IS GROUPED THE WAY A CLOSER READS IT: `lanes.national`, then `lanes.home` (the state they live in), then `lanes.business`. A lender covering both states is a home-lane lender and is listed ONCE. `matches` is unchanged and still the flat tier-ranked list, so no screen breaks; each row gains `lane`, `footprint` and `covers_states`. `resolveMatchState()` still exists and still answers business-then-home, so every existing caller keeps its answer. WHERE THE HOME STATE COMES FROM: `pii_identity.addresses`, written by api/soft-pull-approve.mjs from the consent form's "Enter your current street address, city, state, and ZIP". src/lenders/store.mjs `matchForClient()` now reads that row — the state only, the same direct read src/inquiry-ops/call-scheduler.mjs already makes for the dispute return address — and hands home and business to the matcher. No identity row means an empty home lane, never a block. PROVED: src/lenders/match.test.mjs 43/43 and src/lenders/lenders.test.mjs 13/13, including the owner's own case — an Arizona client with a Florida business against a book of one national, one Arizona-only, one Florida-only and one Texas-only lender matches the first three and refuses Texas with reason `state_ineligible`; the same book with no known state matches all four and refuses none; a lender covering "AZ, FL" appears once. The old single-state path is pinned in a test that shows it losing the Arizona bank, so the defect cannot come back quietly. Unit half 8666 tests / 8663 pass / 0 fail / 3 skip. Database half on a scratch Postgres, first run on a freshly migrated database: 2272 tests / 2256 pass / 15 fail / 1 skip, byte-identical failure names to the same run on origin/main. FOUND AND NOT BUILT: `home_state` is not a field the intake collects — it is not in db/schema/005_client_custom_fields.sql and nothing in the repository writes it — so the home lane rests entirely on the soft-pull personal address, and a client who never went through soft-pull has no home state at all. api/read/tradelines.mjs still carries its own copy of the old one-state chain and was left alone; it is another lane's file. NOT REGENERATED, and correct that it was not: `npm run journeys` was run and produced no diff on any of the eight pages — no route moved and no route's principal gate moved. What changed is which lenders one existing endpoint returns. | An Arizona client with a Florida business was shown Florida banks only, and every bank in the state he actually lives in was silently dropped. | branch feat/two-state-lender-lanes

Rounds 4, 5 and 6 now have their own six openings, six closings and instruction set, carrying EXACTLY Round 3's authority: the same statutory hooks, the same 15-day deletion demand under FCRA 611(a)(5)(A), no new statute, and every mention of the CFPB or a state attorney general still in the future tense so no letter can assert a filing this repository has no record of. `promptPoolRound` ITSELF IS UNCHANGED and still answers 'R4, R5 and R6 are final notices' for its two outside callers (`src/underwrite/prior-outcome.mjs`, `src/repair/round-plan.mjs`); a new `prosePoolRound` answers the narrower which-words question. AFTER: R1 through R6 each write three bureau letters on all three file shapes. SECOND, THE SELF-CONTRADICTING LETTER. A letter whose every claim says the file is RIGHT — this is my one name, this is my one address — still demanded the method of verification for each item and every furnisher's name, address and telephone number. There is no furnisher of a consumer's own name and nothing was verified because nothing was disputed. The confirmation-only substitution existed only in part: MEASURED, 101 of 162 letters (6 rounds x 3 bureaus x 3 regeneration attempts x 3 claim mixes) carried a sentence their own claims did not support, the loudest being the whole Round 2 demand paragraph. Now 0 of 162. A third case is fixed with them and is the common one on a real client: a MIXED letter, one carrying both a real dispute and a confirmation, said 'delete or correct each item' over a list that included the consumer's own correct name — those demands are now scoped to 'each disputed item' and the confirmations asked for separately. THE THREE PERSONAL-INFORMATION RULES CAN FIRE FOR THE FIRST TIME. M2-032 name variants, M2-033 date of birth and M2-034 employment compare the bureau file against what the consumer says is true, `src/metro2/normalize.mjs` marks every `context.consumer` field not-visible because no credit report carries it, and the only production caller never overrode it — so on origin/main those three could not fire at all and the only personal-information rule that ever ran was M2-031, delete addresses older than two cycles. That is age-based cleanup, not the identity-based cleanup the product sells. `src/metro2/diy/consumer-context.mjs` supplies the consumer side, FROM THE VERIFIED IDENTITY ONLY (`src/identity/`, built from the client's uploaded government ID and proof of address) and never from `clients.first_name` or `pii_identity`. That module is another lane's and does not exist yet: it is loaded dynamically and every failure resolves to null, null means unknown, and unknown means the letter makes NO name or address claim at all rather than falling back to the CRM. Date of birth is compared as a DATE, not as a string, so a file reading 1985-03-02 and an ID reading 03/02/1985 are one date and an unreadable one makes no claim. One name claim per bureau, never two: where M2-032 fires the floor's own name claim stands down. A resolver failure keeps today's behaviour (event written, customer null) rather than 500ing the webhook and inviting ClickFunnels to retry. F39 CAUSE TWO — ClickFunnels posts ONE WEBHOOK PER SURVEY SCREEN plus retries (82 posts for 5 real surveys), each under its own ClickFunnels submission id, so the `clickfunnels:<id>:<name>` idempotency key was distinct every time and each post started its own independent text cadence. A repeat post of the same person, same funnel, same event within six hours is now still STORED and still merged onto the customer — the later screens carry answers the first post does not have, and the "Survey Complete" stage depends on the last one, so dropping them would lose the survey — but it is handed to the workflow engine ONLY ONCE (`skipInngest`). Sixteen posts under sixteen different ids now produce exactly one run, counted at the engine itself in `src/adapters/clickfunnels.test.mjs`, not inferred from a flag this code sets on itself. `booking.created` is deliberately excluded: it already has slot-level dedupe and a second booking is a real second appointment. F39, THE HALF THAT STOPS THE RUNS ALREADY ASLEEP — every booking event those runs will wake against was written before any customer was attached, so `hasBooked` in `src/workflows/s-nobook-chase.mjs` also matches the email address (case-insensitive) and the last ten digits of the phone number carried INSIDE the event payload. Scoped to the customer's own company, which the old query was not — an email match without that scope would let one company's booking answer for another's. Proved against a real Postgres in `src/workflows/nobook-chase-historical.pg.test.mjs`, 7/7, because the fake proves the author and only the database proves the SQL. F47 — the "your call is tomorrow" reminder fired within five minutes of booking for four customers, one of whose calls was three days out, and the two-hour reminder went out sixty-eight minutes before the call. The subtraction in `src/workflows/s-04b-booking-reminders.mjs` was never wrong; nothing checked that the moment it produced was still in the FUTURE. An unreadable start time makes an Invalid Date and `sleepUntil` on one wakes immediately; a call booked twenty hours out has a "24 hours before" that is already four hours gone. Both fired at once and both said "tomorrow". A reminder whose moment has passed is now not sent late — it is NOT SENT: `reminderPlan()` refuses an unreadable or already-started appointment outright, skips the 24-hour leg for anything booked inside a day and the 2-hour leg for anything booked inside two hours, and a second check on waking refuses to fire when the clock says the moment has not arrived (a retry or a replay). Tolerance five minutes either side. The confirmation still goes in every case, because it is a receipt for what they booked and not a promise about time. THAT FIRST VERSION WOULD HAVE SWITCHED BOTH REMINDERS OFF, and it was caught and repaired on 2026-09-04 before anything shipped: the plan was worked out in the open rather than inside a recorded step, and the workflow engine re-runs everything outside a step every time it wakes a job — so at the exact moment each reminder came due, the plan was worked out again against that instant, decided the moment had just gone, and sent nothing. Every customer would have received the confirmation and neither reminder. The plan is now computed inside a step and recorded once when the booking arrives, the check made on waking is inside a step too, and `src/workflows/s-04b-booking-reminders.test.mjs` gained three REPLAY tests that drive the workflow the way the engine really drives it — many invocations with the clock moving between them. All three fail against the first version and pass against this one, which is the only reason they are worth having. F49 — the fifteen-minute handoff text arrived reading "so you're not walking in cold — link: ." `src/workflows/ai-set-04-3way-handoff.mjs` called `sendTemplated` with NO context at all while the template interpolates `{{appointment.meeting_location}}`, and an absent tag renders as the empty string. Context alone would not have been enough: ClickFunnels sets `meetingUrl: null` on every booking it takes, so the honest answer is usually "the webhook carried none". Three sources are asked in order — the booking payload, the saved `bookings` row for this appointment or the customer's most recent, and failing both the customer's own portal sign-in page — and the last always answers, so the text always carries a working web address. WHAT IT DOES NOT DO, stated plainly 2026-09-04 rather than left implied: with the wording live in the database today that address is introduced as though it were the link to join the call, and for every ClickFunnels booking the address is the customer's portal sign-in page. The corrected sentence lives in the held copy commit and in `docs/ads/sms-copy-2026-09.md`; nothing in code makes the current sentence honest, so this is named and NOT fixed. The same file also refuses an unreadable start time, and from 2026-09-04 refuses a call that has already started and a booking taken inside the last fifteen minutes — the F47 fault in its second home, which the first version only half fixed, so a booking carrying yesterday's start time used to send "Your call starts in 15 minutes" the instant it arrived. F48 — the closer's pay-link text read "Hi Sim, Fundhub Capital Academy: pay $5,000 <link>", with no sender, no reason and no reference to the call it came out of; beside a dollar amount and a payment link that is indistinguishable from a scam, which is what the owner said on receiving six copies of it. `payLinkSmsBody()` in `src/sales/closer-deck.mjs` now names the sender, the offer, the call it came from and what to do if they are not ready, and carries "Reply STOP to opt out." word for word as every other text does. ONE message either way — the duplicate-send button belongs to another lane and is untouched. F1 — the booking confirmation landed about three minutes after booking against an owner target of sixty seconds, most of it a queued row waiting for a sweep that runs every five minutes. THE SWEEP CADENCE IS NOT TOUCHED, because it is one setting for every kind of message the company sends: instead the TWO confirmation rows, and only those two, ask to be worked immediately by id through `drainMessageNow` (`src/messaging/outbox.mjs`) — the mechanism already built and switched off for exactly this. Every gate still runs inside the dispatcher: the company pause switch, the daily cap, quiet hours, opt-out, the compliance screen. Nothing here can skip one, and the sweep remains the backstop for these two rows and everything else. `src/messaging/outbox.mjs` still said in its own header that nothing called it and that the owner wanted consulting before any immediate-send path went live; on 2026-09-04 that note was replaced with what was actually decided — one caller, two rows, answering the owner's own sixty-second target — so the file and the code no longer contradict each other. THE WORDING IS DRAFTED AND HELD: `docs/ads/sms-copy-2026-09.md` carries the before-and-after of all nine texts plus one false email subject line, and `db/seed/295_sms_copy_2026_09.sql` seeds it. That seed file is the whole of the branch's LAST commit, whose subject begins "HOLD, DROP THE TIP" — identify it by being the tip, because two earlier commits in the history carry a similar "COPY SEED (HOLD)" subject and both are already reverted to nothing. It is held so the owner reads the copy before a customer does. If that commit is dropped the seed file is not in the tree at all and `docs/ads/sms-copy-2026-09.md` is the copy to read; nothing else on the branch depends on it. Not one new template key is introduced, on purpose, so every code change above is correct against the wording live in the database today and dropping that commit breaks nothing — proved by running both halves of the suite with it reverted. Every body ends `Reply STOP to opt out.`, which is exactly what every text the product already sends carries. CORRECTED 2026-09-04: it is NOT the application form's opt-out sentence, which is longer, and the earlier claim that it matched the form word for word was wrong. No message makes any claim about approval, an amount or a credit outcome. FIXTURE HONESTY: `src/workflows/s-04b-booking-reminders.test.mjs` pinned reminders against a hardcoded August date that had slid into the past, so "the reminders fired" proved nothing about a real appointment; those fixtures are now relative to now, and the workflow tests gained a clock, because a sleep that returns instantly cannot prove anything about when a message goes out. No assertion was weakened and none was removed. NOT REGENERATED, and correct that it was not: `npm run journeys` produced no diff on any of the eight pages — nothing here moves a route or a route's principal gate. What moved is how often, when and with what words a message leaves, which that generator does not model. `docs/journeys/booking-notifications-flow.md` is a new page written from the code for exactly that reason (CLAUDE.md §3a.4). | A customer receiving 51 copies of a message that was untrue when it was sent is the product failing in the one place the customer can see it, and every part of it was invisible from inside: the events looked recorded, the workflows looked registered, and the suite was green. | branch fix/r2-w11-notifications

A client can pay before signing or sign before the pull lands, and a chain of ifs that assumes one order gets those people wrong, so `key` is simply the furthest rung reached — booked → soft_pull → call_held → agreement_signed → paid. Pinned by src/http/portal-stage.test.mjs, 7/7, including the walk's own client. F34: the advisor panel read GET /api/dashboard/client, which is staff-gated and 401s for the person whose file it is, so a client ALWAYS saw a blank name; portal-summary now carries the advisor from three sources in order — a name typed on the client record, `custom_fields.cf_funding_advisor_user_id` resolved to a staff row, or the FUNDING ADVISOR who logged a call on the file. EITHER, not both — a repair buyer holds the entitlement before any pull, a DIY client holds the tier before buying anything. It FAILS CLOSED: no org, no client, or a database that will not answer is a no, and the screen's `no-repair-path` class starts ON the body so an unanswered read keeps the form shut. THE SERVER IS THE GATE, NOT THE CSS: api/consent/capture.mjs refuses a CLIENT-principal grant of kind dispute_authorization with 403 not_on_repair_path. F2/F31: A NEW PAYING CLIENT HAD NO WAY IN. api/soft-pull-approve.mjs creates an ACTIVE `accounts` row whose password hash is 32 discarded random bytes — a credential nobody holds, including us — and nothing then sent that person anything, so every buyer walked away with a portal account they could not open and a reset screen telling them to ask an admin. NO NEW ISSUING MACHINERY ANYWHERE: issuePortalLinkForClient() looks the address up and calls requestMagicLink, so the 15-minute TTL, the single use, the hash-only storage and the rate limit are the ones already proven. F37: paintTile flipped the badge to "Included" and left the price under it reading "On your call", so a client who already owned Capital Academy saw their own product priced as a thing to ask an advisor about; the price line now repaints with the lock and keeps the original on `data-price` so a revoked grant restores it. NOT REGENERATED, and correct that it was not: `npm run journeys` was run and produced NO DIFF on any of the eight pages.
