2026-08-31 | white-label | A WHITE-LABEL PARTNER NOW SEES THEIR OWN BRAND ON EVERY CRM SCREEN, NOT FUNDHUB'S. `public/app/shell.js` paints every signed-in screen from `/api/org-brand`, and that endpoint answered with the ORG row for everybody — so a partner signed in and saw Fundhub's colours, type and wordmark, which is the one thing white-label is sold to prevent. `api/org-brand.mjs` now resolves the brand from the PRINCIPAL: a partner principal reads their own `partner_brand` row through `v_partner_brand_effective`, and staff, affiliates and clients read the org row exactly as before. shell.js needed no change to whose brand it paints. THE PARTNER ID IS BOUND FROM THE SESSION AND NOWHERE ELSE — there is no `?partner_id=` on this endpoint, and a partner passing another partner's id in a query string still gets their own brand. It FAILS CLOSED to the org row on every miss: no partner id on the session, no `partners` row, no `partner_brand` row, or a partner whose org does not match the session's. `v_partner_brand_effective` LEFT JOINs and COALESCEs `ink`/`paper` to hardcoded Fundhub values, so it hands back what looks like a full answer for a partner who has saved nothing — the endpoint tests for the row itself with EXISTS rather than trusting the view. THE WORDMARK WAITS FOR APPROVAL AND NOTHING ELSE DOES: `partner_brand.approval_status` is draft/review/approved and the view ignores it completely, so the gate is applied in the handler on one field. Colours and a typeface on a partner's own screens harm nobody and an edit undoes them; a wordmark is an image and an image can carry someone else's registered trademark, so an unapproved one leaves the Fundhub default in place — which is exactly what that partner saw before this change. THE STATUS COLOURS ARE NO LONGER BRAND-DRIVEN, FOR ANYBODY: `paintBrand()` used to overwrite `--alert`, `--warn`, `--ok` and `--info` from ramp stops 0, 1, 3 and 4. Those four are read in 374 places across 43 files under `public/` and every one is a state signal — blocked, behind, healthy — in a regulated consumer-finance product. Nothing constrains a brand ramp to semantically sane stops and a real brand guideline is very often a single-hue gradient, which painted all four as shades of one colour; it had already happened once on a test tenant whose screens went entirely blue. That stayed theoretical only while Fundhub's own sensibly-chosen ramp was the only thing reaching the CRM. It is not theoretical now, and it would land on the PARTNER's own staff. `--ink`, `--paper`, `--spectrum`, `--accent`, `--logo` and both faces stay brand-driven; the four status tokens keep their `fundhub-brand.css` values. THREE PLACES STATED THE OPPOSITE DECISION AND ALL THREE ARE SUPERSEDED IN THIS COMMIT, VISIBLY RATHER THAN DELETED: `public/app/shell.js:2276`, the header of `db/migrations/128_org_brand.sql` (comment only — no SQL changed, and the file says so, because a retired rule stated as current is how the next reader gets it wrong), and `docs/BRAND-THEMING-SPEC.md`. TWO APPROACHES REJECTED AND RECORDED SO NOBODY RE-OPENS THEM: giving each partner their own org (every fulfilment read binds `org_id = staff.org_id`, so Fundhub staff would stop seeing partner clients — an outage, not an upgrade), and rewriting `v_org_brand_effective` (org-keyed, and every partner sits in the shared default org, so it returns one answer for all of them). PROVED: `src/http/org-brand-principal.pg.test.mjs` 15/15 against a real Postgres — a staff GET matched key-for-key against a direct read of `v_org_brand_effective`, partner A never receives partner B's brand, a partner with no row gets the org row whole, affiliate and client unchanged, and the draft/review/approved wordmark split. `src/ui/status-tokens-are-semantic.test.mjs` 5/5 runs the real `paintBrand` against a fake root and fails if any of the four is written again. `e2e/partner-brand-crm.spec.mjs` 5/5 in a real browser on galaxy.html, the screen a partner actually lands on, under a deliberately single-hue ramp — including a reproduction of the old paint so the evidence shows a difference and not a claim. NOT REGENERATED, and it is correct that it was not: `npm run journeys` produced no diff, because the route's principal gate did not move — `/api/org-brand` still admits staff, partner, affiliate and client. What changed is what it ANSWERS WITH, which that generator does not model. | A white-label partner signing in and seeing another company's branding on every screen is the product failing at the thing it is sold for; and shipping that without pinning the status colours would have handed a partner's own staff a screen where blocked and healthy are the same colour. | PR #322

THE BOUNDARY IS THE PRODUCT: column names that look like identity are dropped and counted, and a surviving cell that looks like an SSN, an e-mail or a phone REFUSES THE WHOLE UPLOAD before a byte reaches storage — `decline_autopsy_rows` has no name, SSN, DOB, address, e-mail, phone or account column and no `client_id`, so there is nothing to leak. Pay first, upload second. 2026-08-30 | role-funding-advisor (no journey file changed — `npm run journeys:check` reports docs/journeys up to date; the generator reads routes and gates, and this adds neither) | THE BOARD NOW ANSWERS "WHAT IS WAITING ON ME", AND THE NUMBER IT ANSWERS WITH WAS FIXED BEFORE IT WAS PUT ON SCREEN. A funding advisor with forty clients on one rail could see, per card, that a client had written and nobody had answered, and that a bank had approved with no dollar amount — and nothing anywhere added those up. The only figures on the whole screen were "— cards / — est. / — held", and `.filter-spacer{flex:1}` pushed them to the FAR RIGHT of the third bar, so the leftmost thing on every bar of this screen was a filter. UI-STANDARDS §1 says top-left is the number, never a filter: the screen §12 names as the reference was breaking rule 1 of the standard it is the reference for. THE SOURCE WAS FIXED FIRST, because a beautiful screen on a wrong number is worse than an ugly one. `api/dashboard/pipeline.mjs`'s `approval_amount_missing` over-reported three ways, and each one would have inflated the new headline. (1) AN EXCLUDED APPROVAL IS NOT WAITING — `applications.approval_excluded_at` (db/migrations/272) records, with a name and a time on it, that an approval does not count because it was withdrawn or never used; `client-control-panel.html`'s `isWaitingOnAmount` has always honoured that and this board did not, so the two screens disagreed about the same client and the board kept chasing a question somebody had already answered. (2) THE ROUND — the EXISTS matched every application the client had ever had, so one blank amount left behind in round 1 kept flagging through rounds 2 and 3 forever; it is now scoped to the client's newest round. (3) THE RAIL — one CARDS_SQL serves all eight boards, so the same client's Sales card and Inquiry Removal card carried a funding chip nobody looking at those boards can act on; the flag is now computed only on `funding_card_stacking` and `funding_altfin`. All three were proven by reverting the handler and watching the new database test fail, then restoring it and watching it pass. WHAT IS ON SCREEN: a headline row between the rail switcher and the filter bar, hard left — "WAITING ON US" as an eyebrow and the count at metric size, then ONE line naming ONE card ("Longest wait: Sam Turner — 10d in stage, New Lead") which scrolls to that card and rings it. "US", NOT "ME": `cards.owner` is never written by production code (src/workflows/cards.mjs:106, api/public/partner-apply.mjs:95 — only demo seeds set it), so every real card reads "unassigned" and a per-person number would be invented. To the right, three counters carrying WORDS — "on a bank", "on the client", "nothing recorded" — each of which filters the board when clicked, and the existing card/money/held figures move up beside them. EXACTLY ONE BUCKET PER CARD: a card can be in Round Submitted AND have a text nobody answered, so "us" wins first and us + bank + client + nothing recorded always equals the card count — if they double-counted the screen would contradict itself on its own first row. THE RULE IS DELIBERATELY NARROW. "Us" is an unanswered inbound message (a real EXISTS over `messages`) or an approval with no amount. "A bank" is the `round_submitted` stage and "the client" is `action_required`, THOSE TWO KEYS AND NO OTHERS — every other stage on every rail answers "nothing recorded", which is stated on screen as exactly that and never as "nothing to do". Guessing a party from a stage name nobody agreed on is the kind of number that looks real and is not. A DASH IS NOT A ZERO: before a rail answers and after a rail fails to answer, every figure is "—" and the line underneath says the number is unknown, because a 0 would tell an advisor nothing needs them, which is the one thing an unanswered read can never prove. ALSO: "Waiting on" joins the filter panel as a fifth select (including "Us — an approval amount", so the flag that has been on every card since 2026-08-29 is finally filterable), Sort gains "Amount needed first", and the age filter now reads the raw `entered_at` off the card instead of re-parsing the ROUNDED words it had already printed — "over 3 days" was being decided by a number rounded to the nearest hour or day before anything compared it. REFUSED, ON PURPOSE, EACH WITH A REASON: the dead "Held only" filter was left alone (wiring it needs `round_hold_reason`, which is funding-tier data behind Gate B, and removing it is the owner's call — either way it is a decision, not a detail); `read-signals.mjs`'s hardcoded `ready` and `total_approved` nulls stay honest blanks (a SUM over a table with no rows is 0, and 0 there would read as "no bank has ever approved anything"); and no "gone quiet" counter was built, because the endpoint returns two booleans and no last-message timestamp, so "quiet for 9 days" cannot be said today. UI-STANDARDS §12.6 WAS CORRECTED IN THE SAME CHANGE: both examples it gave of "status is never colour alone" — `.card.held` and `.hold-badge` — are real CSS that has never once painted, because nothing adds either class; the section now cites the live headline counters and `.c-needs-amount`, and says outright that an example which has never run cannot show that it works. NO NEW PAGE, SCREEN, TAB, MENU ROW, ROUTE, ENDPOINT OR MIGRATION. No font size is written on this screen — the metric is `.big` and the label is `.eyebrow`, so `fundhub-brand.css:189-204` hands both their sizes for free and pipeline.html still spends no escape hatch. `--fh-maxw` untouched, no width cap added, no empty-state wording changed. MEASURED on macOS, worktree `wf_6d2ee4ef-d09-7`, branch `feat/pipeline-waiting-on-triage` off main at 6e14b85c. Lint clean, 1611 files. Unit phase 7326 tests / 7313 pass / 10 fail / 3 skipped, against the same worktree with this diff stashed 7315 / 7302 / 10 fail / 3 skipped — THE SAME TEN FAILURES BY NAME, none new, and 26 tests added. `npm test` never reaches the database phase (`scripts/run-suite.mjs` exits after the unit phase when it is red, and it is red on main), so the pg files were run by hand at concurrency 1 exactly as the runner would, against a scratch Postgres 16.14 built for this work with all 219 migrations applied to it empty and dropped afterwards: 1956 tests / 28 fail / 1 skipped, against 1955 / 28 fail / 1 skipped with the diff stashed — the identical 28, every one a partner-isolation / row-level-security suite, which is the class CLAUDE.md §12 says fails when the connection role owns the database. It is worth knowing that this is the ordinary way a case becomes Escalated here (src/inquiry-removal/cases.mjs turns a failed call into Escalated, and by then the call has fired), so in practice the new sentence covers Blocked and little else. 2026-08-30 | role-funding-advisor (no journey file changed — the generator reads routes and gates, and this adds neither) | A ROUND CAN NO LONGER BE MARKED FUNDED WHILE A BANK APPROVAL ON IT HAS NO DOLLAR AMOUNT. Since 2026-08-30 (PR #304) the success fee is a percent of CONFIRMED APPROVALS — Approved applications carrying a recorded amount (docs/CLOSEOUT-FEE-BASIS.md) — so an approval nobody typed a number into is worth nothing on the bill, and once the round closed nobody ever went back for it. That was pure revenue leakage: the screen already warned "1 bank approval is still waiting on its dollar amount" but nothing stopped the round closing anyway. `guardFundedAmount` (src/funding/card-stacking-rounds.mjs), which already refused a funded round with no money at all, now refuses FIRST with `approval_amounts_missing` and NAMES THE BANKS still blank, so the advisor knows exactly what to go and fill in. Server-side only — the board's drag already calls it — and sending a funded amount does not override it, because the blank approval is the problem and a funded amount does not answer it. THE WAY OUT, AND WHY IT IS A FLAG AND NOT A STATUS: a bank can approve and the client never use it, or an approval can be withdrawn, so a hard block with no escape would deadlock a real round on one dead row and turn a revenue guard into a support ticket. New columns `applications.approval_excluded_at / _by / _reason` (db/migrations/272_approval_excluded.sql) record that an approval does not count, with WHO and WHEN, plus an `application_decisions` row exactly as every other decision writes one. `applications.status` is deliberately left at 'Approved': that column is what the BANK said, and src/plays/outcomes.mjs reads approval-vs-denial straight off it ('Approved' to yes, 'Denied' to no), so writing 'Denied' would invert the approval rate and inventing a third status would delete a real bank yes from that report. The audit row carries status NULL so an exclusion never shows up in the bank yes/no history as an outcome at all. An excluded approval also drops out of the fee basis even when it does carry an amount — an approval we are not billing for is not billed at any size. NULL still means unknown and still blocks (CLAUDE.md section 12); nothing defaults to 0, and an explicit zero is still refused as a denial rather than accepted as an approval. A round with no approvals behaves exactly as it did before, and an already-funded round is never re-blocked. Escape wired into the existing /api/applications (no new endpoint, no new screen) and onto the existing lender row in client-control-panel.html as a "Doesn't count" button that asks for a reason. 13 new tests in src/funding/approval-amount-guard.pg.test.mjs. | An approval left blank was money we never invoiced, and a guard with no way out would have deadlocked real rounds. | (this commit)

2026-08-30 | (no journey file changed — recorded deliberately, and the reason matters) | THE SCREEN STANDARD IS NOW WRITTEN DOWN, AND A TEST KEEPS IT TRUE. THE ROOT CAUSE: no settled standard for what a Fundhub screen looks like existed in writing anywhere an agent could see it. On 2026-08-19 two screens were reworked on the same day with OPPOSITE instructions and BOTH agents cited `docs/UI-STANDARDS.md` as their authority — neither was lying, because that document said nothing about what a container looks like at rest, what a topbar must carry, or that a font size written in a screen's own `<style>` is thrown away by the brand file. Each agent filled the gap with a reasonable guess and the guesses did not match. Worse, `docs/UI-STANDARDS.md` was reachable ONLY through a Cursor rule set to `alwaysApply:false`, which Claude Code never loads, so the document an agent was supposed to follow was invisible to it. THREE THINGS, and the smallest is the most important. (1) `docs/UI-STANDARDS.md` gains **§12 THE SCREEN FRAME**, written from what `public/app/pipeline.html` actually does (owner-set: pipeline is the reference for good): the resting container is `#fff` + `1px solid var(--line)` + a radius + `box-shadow:var(--panel-shadow)`; the shadow is NEVER written on a screen because `fundhub-brand.css:130-137` already gives it app-wide to `.card .stat .panel .tabs .editor .fos-panel .kpi-tile .big-tile .calc-panel .compose .ctx-card`, so a new container takes one of those names or joins that list; every OTHER shadow in pipeline.html is an interaction state, not decoration (hover :242, drop target :225, drag ghost :308-309, menu :315-316) — resting elevation is the token and anything stronger is an event; `border:0` on a shadow-list class is forbidden because it paints a floating shadow with no edge; a box that HOLDS boxes takes a flat tint and a hairline, never a shadow (`.col`, pipeline :215); status is never colour alone, because `paintBrand()` in `shell.js:2277-2286` overwrites `--alert/--warn/--ok/--info` from the tenant's own colour ramp and a single-hue white-label brand therefore paints every status identically — pipeline :243-244 survives that with a LEFT STRIPE and a tint, and :263-268 keeps literal amber with the reason written above the rule; the type trap and its ONE escape hatch per screen; and the topbar contract read off `client-control-panel.html:64-84`, including the note that `partner-galaxy.html:79-85` sets `flex-wrap:wrap` AND `overflow:hidden` on the same bar, so nobody copies it. (2) ONE LINE IN `CLAUDE.md` §3 — "Touching anything under `public/app/`? `docs/UI-STANDARDS.md` is law. Read it first." — which is the highest-leverage edit here, because it is what makes the standard reachable at all. (3) NEW `src/ui/screen-standard.test.mjs`, modelled on `src/ui/shell-is-fluid.test.mjs` (same screen discovery, same hand-written CSS walker, same house rule that a failure message must TEACH): no px font-size without `!important` in a shell screen's `<style>`; no topbar that wraps and then clips or pins a height; no shadow-list class dropping its border; and `CLAUDE.md` must still name `docs/UI-STANDARDS.md`. **IT LANDS RED ON PURPOSE — 5 tests, 2 pass, 3 fail — and the failure list IS the burndown for the screens phase.** MEASURED, NOT REPEATED: 210 dead px font-sizes across 10 of 29 shell screens — calendar 58, my-numbers 30, contracts 28, partner-galaxy 28, sales-floor 27, galaxy 24, closer-dashboard 9, consent-capture 4, client-control-panel 1, pipeline 1. The brief's prior figures for my-numbers (31) and contracts (30) counted inline `style=` attributes as well as `<style>` blocks; both are dead, and the inline ones are counted separately (closer-dashboard 6, contracts 2, my-numbers 1) plus `font:` shorthand (consent-capture 21, inquiry-remover 7, pipeline 2). ONE OFFENDER THE BRIEF DID NOT NAME: `ops-admin.html:128` `.aff-body .card{border:0}` keeps the app-wide shadow with no edge, the same fault as `closer-dashboard.html:102` and `:111`. `.gitattributes` is new and carries `docs/journeys/CHANGELOG.md merge=union`, because every branch in this batch appends to this file and would otherwise collide on the first line — "keep every entry" is exactly Git's built-in union driver. NO SCREEN WAS EDITED, no page, screen, tab or menu row was added, no copy was reworded, `--fh-maxw` was not touched, and no migration was added. | Two agents redesigned two screens on the same day from the same rulebook and got opposite answers, because the rulebook did not say what a screen looks like | (this commit)
2026-08-30 | role-funding-advisor (no journey file changed — this is a value on an existing step, not a new step; `npm run journeys:check` reports docs/journeys up to date) | THE BANK'S EMAIL STATES THE APPROVED AMOUNT, THE SYSTEM ALREADY FOUND IT, AND THEN THREW IT AWAY — so a funding advisor retyped by hand a number Fundhub already had, and when they forgot, the approval carried no dollar amount at all. Since PR #304 the success fee is a percent of CONFIRMED approvals — approvals with a recorded amount — so a forgotten box is a bill that never goes out. `src/adapters/mailgun.mjs:96` has always run a dollar pattern over every inbound bank email; it used the result only to nudge classification confidence and discarded the figure. Two things upstream destroyed the evidence as well: the event carried no body text at all, and `src/handlers/comms.mjs` bound the SUBJECT LINE to both the `subject` column and the `body_preview` column of every `bank_inbox` row — so each row showed the same sentence twice, the paragraph stating the amount was gone forever, and because `body_preview` was always populated the screen's `body_preview || classification || received_at` fallback could never reach its second or third option. FIXED IN FOUR PLACES. (1) The adapter now carries the figures: `findDollarAmounts` gathers every DISTINCT dollar figure from the subject and body, as fixed 2dp dollar strings, cents read as whole numbers so $450.10 never becomes $450.09. (2) `bodyPreviewOf` stores a real preview — whitespace flattened, capped at 500 characters with the cut marked, NULL when the email has no text. The WHOLE body is deliberately still not stored: `src/http/router.mjs:110` records what an uncapped body did to a shared production Postgres, and the back half of a forwarded bank statement is account detail nobody asked us to keep. (3) `onMailResponse` writes the preview into `body_preview` instead of a second copy of the subject, and NULL when there is none. (4) `GET /api/read/bank-inbox` returns two named keys out of `raw` — `amount_candidates` and `amount_candidates_found` — and still never returns the `raw` column itself; the endpoint's staff role gate, org gate and client gate are unchanged. ON SCREEN: the figures appear as one-click buttons immediately beside the Approved $ box on each lender row of the client control panel, plus one sentence above the list saying which email they came from. THREE RULES, DELIBERATE. It is a SUGGESTION — clicking fills the box and saves nothing; only Bank yes writes, and only after FHMoneyInput.parseAmount accepts what is in the box. It NEVER PICKS — a bank email routinely states a credit limit, an APR, an annual fee and a minimum payment in one paragraph, so when there is more than one figure the screen says outright "we cannot tell which one is the approval" and offers all of them. And NO FIGURE MEANS NO SUGGESTION, never a zero (CLAUDE.md §12). A DENIED email is not scanned at all — the number in a denial letter is the limit being refused, the most dangerous figure in the mailbox — and the classifier itself is untouched: the negation work from PR #298 that makes "not approved" stay DENIED was not edited, and a test pins its decisions. Database phase run separately (the runner exits after a red unit phase, so pg files skip silently) against a throwaway local Postgres 16.14 with all 218 migrations applied to it empty: 1942 tests / 1913 pass / 28 fail / 1 skipped as the database owner. 26 of those 28 are the partner-isolation suites, which bypass row-level security entirely when the connection is an owner — re-run as `fundhub_app` exactly as CI does, they are 109 tests / 109 pass / 0 fail. The other 2 are in `src/affiliates/economics.pg.test.mjs`, which imports only db.mjs and economics.mjs, fails on its own with nothing else running, and cannot be reached by this change. Playwright 9 new browser tests pass, and funded-amount + proxy-apply + screens-smoke (54 tests) stay green. 2026-08-29 | role-funding-advisor (no diagram changed — the classifier is a value on an existing step, not a new step) | A REJECTION EMAIL FROM A BANK WAS BEING READ AS AN APPROVAL. `src/adapters/mailgun.mjs` matched its keywords with a plain substring test (`lower.includes(kw)`), and the string "not approved" contains the string "approved". APPROVED is the first rule in the list and the first match won, so "Unfortunately, you were not approved for the requested credit limit" classified as APPROVED. That label is not decoration: `src/workflows/f-11-bank-email-event-router.mjs` reads it, and on APPROVED it moves the client's funding card into the `approved` stage of `funding_card_stacking`, sets `employee_next_action` to "Prepare Next Funding Round", and files the task "APPROVAL: log amount + update round". So a client who had just been turned down was recorded as approved, staff were told to prepare the next round on money that did not exist, and the denial follow-up (F-09) never ran because the event never said DENIED. Three more phrasings hit the same fault — "your request was not approved", "we have not approved your application", and any denial whose "not approved" fell across a line break — and the same substring test read "funded" out of "refunded" and "approved" out of "unapproved", so a fee refund also classified as APPROVED. FIXED THREE WAYS, all in the classifier, nothing downstream touched. (1) Keywords now match on WORD BOUNDARIES, and a space inside a keyword matches any run of whitespace so a hard-wrapped denial still matches. (2) NEGATION IS READ BEFORE THE KEYWORD CONTEST: "not approved", "unable to approve", "cannot approve", "will not be approved", "could not fund" and the like drop the APPROVED rule outright and record DENIED — because the leftover APPROVED keywords in a denial letter ("credit limit", "approval") are the letter describing what it is refusing. The gap between the negative and the approval word is a closed list of function words, not a wildcard, so "this does not affect your approved limit" and a "do not reply" footer stay APPROVED. (3) A shorter phrase sitting inside a longer phrase from another rule is discarded as the same words read twice. RULE ORDER WAS DELIBERATELY NOT CHANGED — putting DENIED first only swaps which case breaks, and outcome-beats-process is what the order encodes: an approval that also says "verify your identity" or "please upload your ID" is still an approval, and those are pinned. OWNER-VISIBLE JUDGEMENT CALL: "We approved you for a reduced amount of $2,000" is now COUNTEROFFER, not APPROVED. Both move the card to the same stage, so the stage is not what is at stake — the task is. APPROVED files "log amount + update round", which reads as done; COUNTEROFFER files "review + log offer + next step", which puts a person on the shortfall. A cut-down approval needs the second one. A reduced RATE ("your APR has been reduced") is still APPROVED — it has to be a reduced amount. `classifyBankEmail` and `classifyFull` held two byte-identical copies of this loop; they are now one function with two entry points, so the fix cannot be applied to one and missed on the other. MEASURED: 9 of the assertions in the new tests FAIL on main and PASS on this branch, proven by running both classifiers side by side. 24 new tests, every phrasing one banks actually send. Unit suite 7161 tests / 7151 pass / 7 fail / 3 skipped on this branch vs 7137 / 7127 / 7 fail / 3 skipped on clean main at 4f551ff1 — THE SAME 7 FAILURES BY NAME, none new. The 131 database test files did not run in either measurement: `scripts/run-suite.mjs` exits after the unit phase when it is red, and it is red on main. Run on their own with DATABASE_URL unset they are 675 tests / 57 pass / 0 fail / 618 skipped. macOS, worktree agent-aff7ff77095f78d1a, no database connected. Lint clean. 2026-08-28 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | **COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED** — dispute logic. A MACHINE MAY NO LONGER CARRY A CLIENT INTO ROUND 4. THE HOLE: a client can upload the bureau's reply from the portal, an AI reads it, and when the reading is confident `src/repair/parse-loop.mjs runParseAdvanceLoop` advances the round WITH NOBODY IN THE LOOP — `src/repair/response-agent.test.mjs` "C1 clear letter auto-advances" pins that and it is INTENDED BEHAVIOUR, not a bug, and it was not changed. But that same confident read on a Round 3 item would cross into R4, and R4 is where this branch's own gate releases the CFPB and state attorney general complaints into the client's pack — two documents the consumer signs UNDER PENALTY OF PERJURY. Nothing auto-mails them (`api/repair/send.mjs` requires a staff login) so no paper left the building on its own, but the documents would have been built and handed over on a machine's say-so. OWNER-SET 2026-08-28: auto-advance STAYS for R1→R2 and R2→R3 — those produce another bureau letter, a wrong move costs a letter and is recoverable, and the speed is worth it — and crossing INTO the escalation rounds requires a human confirmation, because a wrong move there produces sworn complaints, which is not the same class of mistake. ONE CHOKE POINT, NOT THREE. `src/metro2/rounds/state.mjs applyItemOutcome` is the only thing in the repository that moves an item up a round; the rule lives there and nowhere else. It gained one option, `humanConfirmed`, which `advanceAfterParse` passes straight through and `confirmParse` computes. On a "verified" answer whose next round is an escalation round, without that flag, the item KEEPS ITS OWN ROUND with status `verified` and `awaiting_human_confirmation: true`. It is not lost: `itemsNeedingEscalation` still reports it, and the parse that produced it is held unconfirmed so a person can act on it. WHAT COUNTS AS A HUMAN: only a real staff id — the same confirmation `src/metro2/inbound/confirm.mjs` already represents. New `isRealStaffId` there is now the single definition, and `src/repair/parse-loop.mjs staffIdOrNull` was a second copy of the same regex and now calls it. The auto path passes `null` or the sentinel `"system_high_confidence"`, and NEITHER BUYS AN ESCALATION — pinned, including through the staff confirm path. `humanConfirmed` must be the literal `true`; `"true"`, `1`, `{}` and every other truthy value are refused. It defaults false, so a caller that says nothing gets the safe answer. THE HELD PARSE GOES TO THE EXISTING QUEUE, AND A REAL BUG WAS FOUND GETTING IT THERE. `confirmParse` now returns `{ok:false, reason:"escalation_needs_human", hold:true}` in exactly the shape the low-confidence hold already used, so `runParseAdvanceLoop`'s existing hold path stores it unconfirmed with no new branch. But `api/repair/exceptions.mjs` — the Inquiry Remover's exceptions screen — listed only parses with `confidence < 0.85`, and an escalation hold is a CONFIDENT read, so it would have sat in the table where NOBODY COULD EVER SEE IT. The parse is now stamped `heldForEscalation` in the existing `parse_json` column (no migration, no new table, no new queue) and that one query was widened to show both kinds. NO NEW QUEUE, NO NEW SCREEN, NO NEW ROUTE. THE RULE DID NOT CHANGE, THE SOURCE OF TRUTH DID: **a claim must be backed by a row, not by an expectation.** NOTHING NEW WAS BUILT TO SEND OR TO STORE. `dispute_letters` (`db/migrations/160_metro2_dispute_engine.sql`) already carries `round` as free text so R4 and R5 already fit, already flips to `status='sent'` when the mail provider accepts a letter (`src/repair/send.mjs`), and `src/metro2/rounds/store.mjs saveLetter` already takes a `target`. NEW `src/metro2/rounds/complaint-filing.mjs` holds the two pieces that genuinely did not exist: WHERE a complaint is mailed, and HOW the record is read back. `loadComplaintFilings` is read-only and filters in SQL on `target IN ('cfpb','state_ag')` AND `status IN ('sent','delivered')` AND the round matching the target, so a row that comes back is by construction a complaint a mail provider accepted. A GENERATED COMPLAINT IS NOT A FILED ONE — `'generated'`, `'ready'`, `'variance_failed'` and `'failed'` all count as not mailed. A corrupt row whose round and target disagree is discarded, not trusted. A database failure returns ZERO filings and is reported, never thrown, so a hiccup makes Round 6 say LESS, never more. `src/metro2/letters/generate.mjs buildLetterText` gained one optional `priorFilings` input, rendered through the same evidence-block mechanism the existing PRIOR BUREAU RESPONSE facts already use — no new letter shape — as `COMPLAINTS ALREADY FILED (evidence):` with one plain sentence per recorded mailing. It is gated to R6 alone, and to a non-empty record: with no record the R6 letter renders EXACTLY as it did before this path existed. `src/repair/analyze.mjs` loads the record only when it is building an R6 letter. The fifty state attorneys general have NO street address here: `AG_BY_STATE` carries an office name and a web portal for five states (TX, CA, FL, NY, IL) and a generated placeholder for the other forty-five, and never a street. **Fifty addresses were not invented** — a wrong address means a complaint the client signed under penalty of perjury is mailed into a void while everyone believes it was filed. So `agPostalAddress()` returns null for every state, `complaintDestination` refuses that send with `ag_postal_address_unknown`, `sendRepairLetters` stops before mailing so NO filing row is written, and Round 6 stays silent about a state AG filing exactly as it does today. FIXED by separating two questions that were tangled into one function. WHAT DOCUMENT IS ROUND N is now `ROUND_LADDER` in `catalog.mjs`: R1 Metro 2 dispute, R2 method of verification, R3 final notice, **R4 CFPB complaint**, **R5 state attorney general complaint**, R6 final notice reissued. WHICH PROSE SHAPE A BUREAU LETTER USES stays `promptPoolRound`, now R4/R5/R6 → R3, because after Round 3 every bureau letter is a final notice and dropping back to the Round 2 question is a step DOWN in authority. R6 — WHAT WAS REUSED AND WHY, STATED PLAINLY: there is no eighth letter type called "escalation final notice" and NONE WAS INVENTED. R6 reuses `R3_FINAL_NOTICE`, because a final notice to the bureau is exactly what R6 is — the last bureau letter, sent when the two regulator complaints have not moved the item either. It is labelled "Final notice, reissued" so it never prints Round 3's title or Round 3's timing. **R6 DOES NOT AND MAY NOT SAY THE COMPLAINTS WERE FILED**, and that is a FINDING, not a gap that was filled: NOTHING IN THIS REPOSITORY RECORDS THAT A CLIENT EVER FILED ONE. The pack ships both complaints undated and unsigned inside `06-complaints-CONDITIONAL/` behind a cover sheet reading DO NOT FILE WITH ROUND 1; the client writes the date, hand-signs the perjury declaration and files them personally, and no table, column, endpoint or workflow here ever hears whether that happened — `dispute_items` records bureau rounds and nothing else. Writing "I have filed a CFPB complaint" into R6 would be the identical defect already fixed on main, where the Round 3 letter told the bureau a CFPB complaint was being filed and no such document had ever been produced. Pinned: no R4–R6 opening, closing, instruction or rendered letter claims a filing happened — every CFPB and attorney-general reference in them is future tense. NO NEW STATUTE WAS WRITTEN; every citation used already existed in `src/metro2/letters/`. FAULT 2 — THE SWORN COMPLAINTS RELEASED ON THE WRONG CONDITION. `src/underwrite/letter-pack.mjs` released the CFPB and state AG complaints whenever the pack had produced ANY dispute letter. A pack writes a Round 1 letter for a client on their first day, so both complaints — signed by the consumer UNDER PENALTY OF PERJURY and saying "I disputed inaccurate information with the consumer reporting agencies" — were handed to clients who had disputed nothing and heard back from nobody. That sworn sentence was false and the federal complaint was out of order. FIXED: the gate is now the round the client is ACTUALLY on, which became knowable when the recorded bureau answer was wired to the pack (`src/underwrite/prior-outcome.mjs`, PR #281). New `reachedEscalation()` requires a recorded answer at R4, R5 or R6. An item only sits at R4 because R1, R2 and R3 were EACH answered "verified" and a human confirmed EACH one — `loadPriorOutcomes` filters in SQL on `status = 'escalated' AND outcome = 'verified'`, a pair only `src/metro2/rounds/state.mjs applyItemOutcome` can set, and only behind the human gate in `src/metro2/inbound/confirm.mjs`. A CLIENT CAN NEVER REACH R4 BY DEFAULT, BY GUESS OR BY EMPTY DATA: an empty list, a missing argument, a null round, "R9", the number 4, "round 4" and a database error all refuse, and a database failure WITHHOLDS the complaints rather than releasing them. The old "dispute letters in the same pack" rule is KEPT as the pack-health check it always really was, and is reported first, so `no_dispute_letters` means the pack broke while `not_escalated` means the client is simply not there yet — the normal state for almost every client. The complaints still ship last, still inside `06-complaints-CONDITIONAL/`, still behind the same cover sheet, still built only by `src/metro2/diy/package.mjs maybeComplaintFiles` — no second copy. NO TEST WAS WEAKENED, SKIPPED OR DELETED: the three assertions that changed (`promptPoolRound("R4")`, `promptPoolRound("R6")`, `priorOutcomeForRound`) pinned the bug itself, and each was replaced with a stronger assertion plus a new one forbidding any round from stepping back down to the Round 2 wording. 2026-08-28 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | **COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED** — dispute logic. UnderwriteIQ could only ever write a Round 1 dispute letter. Rounds 2 and 3 were gated on one field on each account, `priorOutcome`, which `src/underwrite/letter-pack.mjs` read and **nothing in this repository ever wrote**. The vendor letter writer (`vendor/underwriteiq-full/api/lite/letter-generator.js`, `getAccountsForRound`) hands out the Round 2 letter only to accounts whose `priorOutcome` is exactly `"verified"` and the Round 3 letter only to `"verified_round2"`, so both matched zero accounts every time. That is why the escalation ladder's top rung was out of reach: the CFPB and state attorney general complaints ship inside `06-complaints-CONDITIONAL/` behind a cover sheet reading DO NOT FILE WITH ROUND 1 — send only after Round 3 is done, and Round 3 could not be reached. NOTHING NEW WAS BUILT TO CAPTURE A BUREAU ANSWER, because a capture path already existed and works: staff post a scanned bureau letter to `/api/repair/inbound-mail`, `src/metro2/inbound/parse-response.mjs` reads it, a human confirms it (`src/metro2/inbound/confirm.mjs`, or `/api/repair/exceptions` when confidence is low), `src/metro2/rounds/state.mjs applyItemOutcome` moves the account up a round, and `src/repair/parse-loop.mjs persistAdvancedItems` writes the answer to `dispute_items`. What was missing was one wire from that stored answer to the letter pack's gate. New file `src/underwrite/prior-outcome.mjs` reads it — read-only, never writes — and `buildLetterPackForClient` now makes a third read for it. THE GATE: an account advances only when all three of `status = 'escalated'`, `outcome = 'verified'` and `round = R2..R6` are true in the database at once, which only `applyItemOutcome` can produce and only from a confirmed answer. An unanswered account is `open` or `sent` and never advances; an account that hit the program's round cap is `closed` and never advances; a deleted, updated or unaddressed account is finished and never advances. Matching is strict on purpose — same bureau, then the last four digits of the account number; a creditor name alone is accepted only when the stored record carries no account number AND exactly one account at that bureau bears that name, so two cards from one bank can never both escalate off one answer. Rounds 4–6 reuse the Round 2 / Round 3 letter shapes through the repository's existing `promptPoolRound` rather than a second rule being invented. Proven by `src/underwrite/prior-outcome.test.mjs` (18 tests): with no recorded answer the pack still writes Round 1 and only Round 1 — that regression is pinned first — and with a recorded answer real `ex_round2.pdf` and `ex_round3.pdf` files come out. WHAT A HUMAN STILL DOES BY HAND: nothing in this change mails a bureau letter or reads a bureau reply. Someone still has to put the bureau's scanned reply into `/api/repair/inbound-mail`, and confirm it if the reader is unsure. LEFT AS IT WAS, NOT FIXED HERE: two accounts pre-date this work — the Round 3 letter text still tells the bureau a CFPB complaint is being filed, and the complaint pair is still gated on the pack containing at least one dispute letter rather than on Round 3 having actually been reached. NOT REGENERATED, AND WHY: `docs/journeys` is stale by the same 9 files before and after this change; the journey extractor reads only `netlify/functions/api.mjs`, `src/http/read-api.mjs`, `src/http/partner-read-api.mjs` and `src/http/dashboard-auth.mjs`, this change touches none of them and adds no route, so regenerating here would sweep in another change's undocumented `/api/public/optimize` routes. | Rounds 2 and 3 were dead code the owner had paid for, and the escalation ladder's last rung could not be reached in normal use | (this commit)
2026-08-28 | (changelog repair, no code) | Restored 212 lines of history this branch had truncated. Cause, recorded so it is not repeated: a changelog append written as `open(p,'w').write(new + open(p).read())` — `open(p,'w')` truncates the file BEFORE the read runs, so the read returns empty and only the new line survives. Read first, then open for write. | Self-inflicted, caught by an adversarial review before merge | (this commit)

2026-08-28 | all actuals regen (no route/gate change) | Regenerated after restoring two imports the 2026-08-27 merge dropped: `logoPathOrPlaceholder` in src/lenders/resolve-logo.mjs and `linesForEngine` in api/read/underwrite.mjs. The second meant the UnderwriteIQ read endpoint threw on every request, so the generated actuals had drifted from what the code really does. | Owner: remove sim data / fix what the merge broke | (this commit)
2026-08-27 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | Kills the last sideways page scroll in the CRM and makes Pipeline's board fit the owner's 2560 monitor. TWO SCREENS, ONE BUG: campaign-manager (381px of sideways scroll at 1440) and social-studio (195px) were not two problems. shell.js mountChip builds the session chip with its geometry as an INLINE style including flex-shrink:0, measured 558px wide, and placeInHeader drops it into whatever top bar the screen already has. In a bar that is already full, an unshrinkable 558px child pushes the whole PAGE sideways. The four declarations that let it shrink already existed but sat inside the 860px phone block, so they never applied on a desktop; nothing about the problem is phone-shaped, so they are hoisted to apply at every width. That alone also fixed creative-factory, which was scrolling 144px at 1280 and had not been reported. SHRINKING ALONE WAS NOT ENOUGH, and the second half matters: at 1280 the chip obediently collapsed to 26px while its own children kept their intrinsic widths, so a 106px label still hung 101px off the page AND the chip had become unreadable. Squeezing harder makes both worse. So a bar that holds the chip may now WRAP, letting the chip drop to its own row at a readable width. Scoped with :has() to bars that actually contain it, and covering BOTH containers shell.js can choose — `.topbar` and `.topbar-right` (11 screens have the latter; they measure clean today, and covering only the broken one is the half-fix that leaves the other as a landmine). PIPELINE AT 2560: ten columns at the 238px minimum need 2498px and only 2332px exists, so the board scrolled 166px on the owner's monitor at 100% zoom. From 2560 up the minimum may relax to 216px, and flex-grow then pushes the columns back out to fill what is actually there — 220px at 2560, 308px at 3440. Deliberately NOT lowered below 2560: there the board scrolls either way, so narrowing the cards would cost laptop users width and buy nothing. Verified live on all 29 shell screens at 1280 / 1440 / 2560 / 3440: 29/29 clean at every width, zero page-level sideways scroll anywhere, and pipeline board scroll 0 at both 2560 and 3440. Before this change: 26/29 at 1280 and 27/29 at 1440. | Owner-set 2026-08-27: every tab must look the same and none of them may scroll sideways | (this commit)
2026-08-27 | role-sales-manager, role-closer (no route/gate change) | Pipeline's board columns now STRETCH to fill a wide window instead of stopping short of the right edge (owner-set). Was `.col{width:238px;flex-shrink:0}`, which pinned the board at 2498px however wide the monitor got — measured 714px of empty board to the right of the last column at 3440. Now `flex:1 1 238px;min-width:238px`: columns share the free space when there is any and never shrink below the width a card was designed for. `width:max-content` REMOVED from `.board` and it must not come back — with a growable column it stops meaning 'as wide as the columns', because intrinsic sizing resolves each item at its CONTENT max-content; tried first and measured a 3501px board with 338px columns at every viewport, scrolling even at 3440. The board now takes its normal auto width (the scroll wrap's), which is what gives the columns space to grow into. A 4px `::after` spacer restores the trailing gap after the last column that the old max-content used to give via padding. Measured live at four widths: 3440 board 3212 / columns 308 / no scroll and no empty board; 2560 columns 238 and 166px of board scroll; 1440 columns 238 and 1286px of board scroll; 1280 columns 238 and 1446px — the last three identical to before, so nothing regressed on a narrower screen. Page-level sideways scroll stayed 0 at every width. NOTE for the owner: at 2560 at 100% zoom ten 238px columns need 2498px and only 2332px exists, so the board still scrolls 166px there — exactly as it did before. It fills once the window is wider than that, which is what zooming out does. The 238px minimum was NOT lowered to force a fit, because that is a card-design decision, not a layout one. | Owner-set 2026-08-27: the page was fluid but the board was not, so Pipeline alone looked unfinished on a wide monitor | (this commit)
2026-08-27 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | Locks the fluid layout in so it cannot drift back. New guard `src/ui/shell-is-fluid.test.mjs` (7 tests) fails if any screen re-caps its own page column, if the crm-sidebar.css shell rule stops saying `max-width:none !important`, if `.fh-maxw` regains a cap, if the `--fh-maxw` token is deleted (client-portal.html reads it for its 780px reading column), or if the 860px phone pin is removed. The guard immediately caught two real landmines and they are removed in the same commit: `my-numbers.html` `.shell{max-width:1240px;margin:0 auto}` and `sales-floor.html` `.shell{max-width:1280px;margin:0 auto}`. Both were already DEAD — crm-sidebar.css overrode them with !important — but anyone deleting the shell rule believing it to be 'the cap' would have got those two numbers back and left those screens narrow while every other tab filled the monitor. Audited all 36 pages in public/app: 29 carry the shell and every one is fluid; of the remaining 7, only `client-portal.html` is referenced by the staff nav and its 780px reading column is deliberate. `soft-pull-approve.html` keeps its 560px form width — a client approval form, not a tab. Verified on production after the merge of #248: all five sampled screens 0 dead margin at 1440/2560/3440, and every major layout block on all 29 screens grows when the window widens. KNOWN AND NOT CHANGED: the Pipeline board's columns are a fixed 238px each (10 columns = 2498px), so on a 3440 window the board stops 714px short of the right edge — the page is fluid, the board's columns are not. That is a design decision for the owner, not a bug in the shell. | Owner-set 2026-08-27: every tab must look the same when switching between them, and must stay that way without anyone policing it by hand | (this commit)
2026-08-27 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | Follow-on to the full-width change, same day. THREE screens mount the shell's account row (Journeys, My Numbers, Sales Floor) and on Journeys it was stealing 718px of the window. Root cause in shell.js placeInHeader: the host was picked with one grouped `querySelector(".shell, .main, .content, main, .app-shell, .app")`, and a selector list returns the first match in DOCUMENT order, not list order. `.app` is an ANCESTOR of `.shell`, so the grouped form always resolved to `.app` and never to the content column it was written to prefer. Harmless while `.app` is display:block (My Numbers and Sales Floor just get a full-width block row), but Journeys sets `.app{display:flex}`, so the row became a flex ITEM sitting between the rail and the content. Measured live: Journeys painted its content in a 494px column at 1440 and left a 1065px gap at 3440. Fixed by trying the hosts in order, innermost first. After: all three screens 0 gap on both sides, content 1212px at 1440 and 3212px at 3440, no sideways scroll. Also in this change, unrelated to layout: tsconfig.json ADDED — CLAUDE.md §6 requires `npx tsc --noEmit` to pass and there was no tsconfig at all, so tsc found no inputs, printed its help and exited 0; the gate had been passing by checking nothing for its whole life. checkJs is off on purpose (measured 5393 errors if enabled wholesale), files opt in with `// @ts-check`, and money.mjs and chunk.mjs are opted in as the first two. CLAUDE.md §12 updated with the first real database-suite measurement: 6867 tests, 6866 pass, 0 skipped, and the one failure is a connection-role artifact that passes as fundhub_app. | Journeys was unusable wide — content in a 494px column — and two 'done' gates were not checking anything | (this commit)
2026-08-27 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | CRM content now fills the browser window at any zoom instead of stopping at 1800px and centring. Three rules applied the same cap and all three had to go: `.fh-maxw` in fundhub-brand.css (max-width and the auto inline margin dropped, width:100% kept), the shell rule at crm-sidebar.css that made the cap global on 2026-08-18 (inverted to `max-width:none !important` rather than deleted — deleting it would have handed sales-floor.html its own hardcoded 1280px and my-numbers.html its 1240px back, leaving those two narrow while every other screen filled the monitor), and `#fosWrap` in finance-os.html, an ID selector the shell rule cannot reach, which would otherwise have been the single screen still capped. margin-inline is pinned to 0 rather than removed because those same screens set `margin:0 auto` and an auto cross-axis margin cancels flex stretch, which shrink-wraps a wrapper to its widest child. The `--fh-maxw` token is deliberately KEPT at 1800px though nothing caps with it any more: client-portal.html reads it as `min(780px, var(--fh-maxw))` to hold the client's 780px reading column, and deleting the token makes that min() invalid, which drops max-width to none and blows the client portal full width. The phone pin (`max-width:100%` under @media max-width:860px, UI-STANDARDS §11 no sideways scroll) and the 228px rail offset (`padding-left:var(--fh-side-w)`) are both untouched. docs/UI-STANDARDS.md §1 updated in the same commit: "Max content width 1800px, centered" is replaced by "Content fills the window. No max width.", keeping the note on why 1280 was abandoned. | Owner-set 2026-08-27: zooming out on a 2560px monitor to get more room returned none — content stopped growing and every extra pixel became empty side margin (532px dead at 2560, 1412px at 3440). Raising the cap 1280->1800 on 2026-08-17 had not fixed that; removing it does. | (this commit)
2026-08-27 | role-funding-advisor, role-owner (no route/gate change) | The Fulfillment "Needs Pull" and "Needs Consent" tiles were silently dropping almost every client. The fraud check compared TRIM(custom_fields->>'round_hold_reason') to 'Fraud Alert' with no COALESCE, so a client with no hold reason - the common case - produced NULL, which poisoned the whole OR, made NOT NULL null, and COUNT(*) FILTER skipped the row. Both tiles read 0 with a client that qualified on every other term. Adding COALESCE fixes it; a test now pins that a qualifying client must land in one of the two counts. | Owner: Needs Pull tile undercounted | (this commit)

No max width.", keeping the note on why 1280 was abandoned. | Owner-set 2026-08-27: zooming out on a 2560px monitor to get more room returned none — content stopped growing and every extra pixel became empty side margin (532px dead at 2560, 1412px at 3440). 2026-08-19 | role-owner, role-sales-manager, role-closer, role-funding-advisor, role-inquiry-remover (no route, no gate, and no `-actual.md` moved) | **COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED** — four numbers on the Fulfillment lens were telling lies, found by an adversary pass over the entries below. Same two screens, nothing else touched. **1. The tile disagreed with the row beneath it.** The "Needs Pull" tile counted a client as waiting on a credit pull whenever they had paid and had no credit report row on file. The chip on the row used one more test that the tile did not: a client whose credit status is already recorded as Complete is not waiting on anything. Measured on real Postgres, the tile said 4 while only 2 of those clients got the chip. The tile now uses the same rule as the chip, and the "waiting on written permission" sentence under it uses it too, so no client falls out of both counts. **2. Demo rows counted as real ones everywhere except credit.** Practice data is marked with a demo flag. The credit read always skipped it; the other reads did not. So with Demo Mode OFF, a practice task added a real client to "Action Needed" and painted a warning pill on their row, and a practice funding round could put a funding step on their file. Every read that can skip practice rows now does — tasks, funding rounds, funding cards, documents and inquiry cases. Two cannot: the dispute tables have no demo flag, and the invoice balance view does not carry the one on invoices; both would need a migration and this layer writes no schema, so both are recorded in the code rather than left silent. **Trade-off, recorded not hidden:** with Demo Mode ON, a practice client's own practice rows are now skipped too, so their file shows fewer warnings than before. That is the same behaviour the credit read has always had. **3. A failed read could read as "nothing is wrong with this file".** The warning list is built from three reads. If any one of them refused, the list came back empty and nothing was marked as unread — and an empty warning list is the whole test for "Ready to Fund". So an unreachable table could turn into a step saying the file is clear to fund. A read that fails now says so, that one step answers "we do not know", and the screen falls back to today's display. It does not take away an answer that never needed the list: a client whose step is "Get Consent" still gets it, and a repair-only client still correctly shows no funding step. **4. A tile counting everybody sat over a list that stops short.** "Total clients" counts the whole book while the list below it asks for one page. At 47 clients they match; above the page size the screen made a claim it could not back up. The tile now carries one sentence — "The list below shows 200 of these 431." — and only when the two numbers really disagree. No seventh tile, no paging control. **Read and display only:** no write, no migration, no column, no route, no screen, no tab, no menu row, no workflow touched. **Proof:** every fix has a test that fails against the code as it was — checked by putting each defect back and watching the test fail. `src/fulfillment/read-signals.pg.test.mjs` gains a fixture with a practice task and practice funding round on a REAL client, and a third org proving the tile and the chip agree on "Complete"; `src/fulfillment/read-signals.test.mjs` gains four tests that force the warning read to refuse; `src/fulfillment/next-action.test.mjs` gains five more on the same rule; `src/http/pipeline-screen.test.mjs` gains four on the new sentence. No test weakened, none deleted. | A tile that disagrees with the row under it is worse than no tile, practice data was being counted as real with Demo Mode off, and a failed read was coming out as a clean bill of health. | uncommitted (worktree `feat/fulfillment-layer-phase1`)
2026-08-19 | role-owner, role-sales-manager, role-closer, role-funding-advisor, role-inquiry-remover (no route, no gate, and no `-actual.md` moved) | **COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED** — three display defects fixed on the same two screens as the two entries directly below, and nothing else touched. **1. The big Next Action line no longer goes blank.** When the system worked a file out and found no step that applies, the line went empty and the page painted a bare em dash — with the grey "Saved on the record: Pull CRS" line sitting right underneath it. A heading, a dash, then the forbidden words: it read as though the dash were hiding the real instruction. The line now reads "No step applies right now." All three answers are plain sentences and no two read the same: the step itself, "No step applies right now.", or "Not worked out yet." The saved value stays exactly where Chris approved it — a quiet line underneath, labelled as what is on the record, never as an instruction. **2. Four money displays that were already on the screen are back to what they printed on `main`.** Prequal, Total Approved and the two income-per-year lines had been switched onto the new shared money rule, which changed them: a recorded zero became "$0" where it had been a dash, and a negative became "$-500". Nobody asked for either. They are back on `main`'s rule byte for byte. The one shared whole-dollars rule stays for the two NEW amounts — this page's funding-round approved amount and the pipeline lens's — so the $50,000-reading-as-$500 defect the entry below fixed stays fixed. **Whether a recorded zero on those old tiles should read "$0" or a dash is Chris's call and was deliberately not made here**; it reads as a dash, which is what it read before this work. **3. Funding money could reach a client who only bought credit repair.** A `funding_rounds` row carries an approved amount, and both screens painted the newest one for anybody who had one, so a REPAIR_ONLY client could read "Round 1 · approved · approved $75,000". No chip is involved, so Gate B's chip guard never saw it. **Gate B now covers the money as well as the chip, through the same helper and the same whitelist** — `fundingChipsAllowed()` in `src/fulfillment/next-action.mjs`, reading `src/config/product-path.mjs`. A round comes back only on the three funding tiers; repair-only, an unrecognised tier and no tier at all get nothing, and that refusal does NOT mark the file as unread — it is the right answer, not a failure. The Client Control Panel's round block also stopped reading the raw newest row as a fallback, which was the way around the gate; it now shows the worked-out round or dashes, and the round number, status and hold reason are still on the page in the tiles that always showed them. **Read and display only:** no write, no migration, no column, no route, no screen, no tab, no menu row, no workflow touched. `npm run journeys` still generates every page byte-identical, the correct result for a change that adds no route and no gate. **Proof:** `src/fulfillment/next-action.test.mjs` gains five tests that fail if a repair-only client is handed a funding round, one of them asserting the round and the chip agree tier for tier so there can only ever be one whitelist; `src/http/client-panel-screen.test.mjs` gains three tests pinning the old tiles to `main`'s rule and three that fail if either screen reads a raw funding-round row again, and the one test that asserted the old blank slot now asserts the plain words. No test weakened, none deleted. Both screens were also loaded in a real browser: a repair-only client carrying a $75,000 round row shows dashes in the round block and no round line on the pipeline lens, while a full-funding client still shows Round 1 · approved · $75,000. | A bare dash above the words "Pull CRS" is the worst way that line could read, tiles nobody asked to change had changed, and funding money was reaching people who never bought funding. | uncommitted (worktree `feat/fulfillment-layer-phase1`)
2026-08-19 | role-owner, role-sales-manager, role-closer, role-funding-advisor, role-inquiry-remover (no route, no gate, and no `-actual.md` moved) | **COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED** — fixes two defects an adversary pass found in the Fulfillment Layer Phase 1 entry directly below, both on the same two screens and nowhere else. **1. The big Next Action line no longer shows the value saved on the record.** It used to, whenever the answer could not be worked out — and that is exactly the case where consent could not be established, while the saved value is written with no consent check at all and is the literal words "Pull CRS" in `src/workflows/s-06-post-call-funding-purchased.mjs` and `src/workflows/c-05-pre-funding-review.mjs`. A client with no recorded consent could therefore be shown "Pull CRS" on screen, which is the compliance gate Chris called non-negotiable. The big line now only ever shows a step the system worked out; when nothing was worked out it says "Not worked out yet." in plain words. **Nothing is lost:** the saved value moves to a quiet grey line underneath reading "Saved on the record: Pull CRS", so staff keep the information and simply stop being told to act on it. This corrects the sentence in the entry below that says the panel shows the value last saved on the record — it does not any more, and that was the leak. **2. The two screens agreed to disagree about money.** `public/app/pipeline.html` divided a number by a hundred and `public/app/client-control-panel.html` did not, so the same amount could print as $50,000 on one screen and $500 on the other. The real unit was traced, not guessed: `funding_rounds.approved_amount` is a `numeric(14,2)` column and reaches the browser as the text "50000.00", meaning fifty thousand dollars, and `custom_fields.total_funding_estimate` is saved in whole dollars as a plain number. **Both are dollars.** They are exceptions to the integer-cents rule in `CLAUDE.md` §12, which describes `src/commissions/money.mjs`; that module converts into cents at its own door (`src/commissions/calculate.mjs` calls `toCents()` on a funded amount), so cents never leave the commission maths. Both screens now use the one dollars rule, written in a comment beside each formatter. Nothing on file still prints as an em dash and never as $0; a zero that really is recorded prints as $0 on both, because hiding a recorded zero behind a dash calls a known fact unknown. **Read and display only, same as the entry below:** no write, no migration, no column, no route, no screen, no tab, no menu row, no workflow touched. 2026-08-19 | role-owner, role-sales-manager, role-closer, role-funding-advisor, role-inquiry-remover (no route, no gate, and no `-actual.md` moved) | **COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED** — the Fulfillment Layer, Phase 1, built on the Phase 0 mapping at `docs/workflows/fulfillment-layer-2026-08-19.md` and Chris's option A. **Read and display only: no INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE anywhere, no migration, no new table, no new column, no new route, no new screen, no new tab, no new menu row, and no workflow or job touched.** **`npm run journeys` was re-run and every generated page came back byte-identical, which is the correct result** — these pages track routes and the gate on each one, and this work adds neither; it adds derived fields to two read endpoints that already existed and paints them on two screens that already existed. That is the same outcome the 2026-08-19 role-inquiry-remover entry below recorded for the same reason. **What a staff member now sees.** Chris's Employee Next Action field is blank on 39 of 47 client records, so the two screens stop showing that saved stamp on its own and instead work the answer out from the client's own records: `src/fulfillment/read-signals.mjs` gathers the evidence with SELECTs only and `src/fulfillment/next-action.mjs` decides, with no database access of its own so the same answer reaches every screen from one rule instead of three copies. The Client Control Panel (`public/app/client-control-panel.html`) gains one read-only block — next action with the sentence explaining why, the active blockers, and where the funding round stands with its finalized state — and its existing "Next action" line is now driven by the same worked-out answer so the two lines on one page cannot disagree. The pipeline board (`public/app/pipeline.html`) gains a **Board / Fulfillment switch on the filter bar it already had**; Board is the default and is byte-for-byte the screen that shipped before, and turning the lens on only sets `hidden` on the board row — no card, column, listener, filter or scroll position is rebuilt, re-queried or re-sorted, and turning it off restores every one of them, including the previous hidden state of the search box, the filter button, its chips and the summary. The lens lists the clients with a next-action chip on each row, the six rollup tiles, and click-a-chip-to-filter. **The order is Chris's, first match wins, and nothing in the code implies it** (Phase 0 §4 says so plainly): Clear Fraud Alert, Get Consent, Pull CRS, Remove Inquiries, Collect Documents, Review Disputes, Review Funding File, Prepare Next Round, Apply for Funding, Ready to Fund. "Lock Fee" is deliberately not a queue chip — money-side signal only — and "File Prep" is deliberately dropped. **TWO COMPLIANCE GATES, EACH BUILT TWICE.** GATE A: a client with no live written permission is never shown "Pull CRS"; their step is "Get Consent", which is ranked above the pull so first-match-wins refuses it, AND `guardConsentBeforePull()` refuses it again on the final answer while knowing nothing about the order, so reordering the list cannot defeat it. This closes Phase 0 defect 2, where the pull button was correctly greyed out but the words above it were not checked at all, so staff read an instruction the system would then refuse — the screen and the button now read the same consent through the same `consentStatus()` call `src/finance/soft-pulls.mjs` gates the pull on, and `src/consent/index.mjs` exports its validity predicate (additive, behaviour unchanged) so a 500-client list cannot fork the rule into a second hand-typed copy. GATE B: a repair-only client is never shown a funding chip — Review Funding File, Prepare Next Round, Apply for Funding, Ready to Fund — enforced by the chip's own predicate AND again by `guardFundingProduct()`, which reads its own separately written list of the four and fails closed on an unknown, missing or unrecognised product type. That closes Phase 0 defect 1 for the display, where a repair-only client is being told to "Apply for Funding" today. Both gates, both halves, have tests that fail if the guard is removed (`src/fulfillment/next-action.test.mjs`, 78 tests). **When a signal cannot be read the screen falls back to today's display — never a blank, never a crash, never a guess.** Every derivation is wrapped; the four new fields are absent from the reply rather than blank; an unknown answer sets `next_action_degraded` and the panel says in words that this is the value last saved on the record. Blank survives as blank: the derivation reads the raw `custom_fields` blob and the raw tag array rather than the response mapper, because that mapper turns a missing `crs_paid` into `false` (Phase 0 defect 7) and "we do not know" is not "no". Money is passed through exactly as Postgres hands it over and a NULL approved amount stays a dash — never $0. **Two of the six tiles are `null` on purpose, and that is the answer, not a gap**: "Ready" and "Total Approved" have no honest source (Phase 0 §5), so each carries a sentence saying so rather than a zero, which would be a claim nobody can stand behind; "Total Prequal" is shown beside the number of clients it came from, because Phase 0 measured the whole company total coming from one client out of 47. **GAPS RECORDED, NOT RECONCILED, and no `-intended.md` was edited:** (1) **no `-intended.md` file names either screen at all** — the Client Control Panel and the pipeline board appear in none of the eight, only `role-inquiry-remover-intended.md` describes a screen and that screen is the Specialist desk — so nothing hand-written says what a next action should say, what order the chips belong in, or that a permission gate or a product-type gate should exist there; Chris's order and both gates are owner-set direction from this batch, not journey-derived, and an agent may not write them into the intended file; (2) **"Ready to Fund" is a step the code now has that no intended journey and no Phase 0 finding authorises** — Phase 0 row 10 recorded zero matches anywhere in the system for it; it was added by the build lead, ranked last, and given a derived meaning (nothing blocking, newest round not on hold, paperwork complete) that is a definition nobody wrote down, and it needs Chris to say whether that is what he means; (3) **the lens shows an eleventh chip that is not on Chris's list** — "Not enough information", the honest label for a client whose next step could not be worked out; it is clickable and filterable like the other ten, and Chris has not seen it; (4) **`role-sales-manager` and `role-inquiry-remover` can now be told on screen to "Get Consent" and have no route to do it** — both reach `/api/dashboard/client` and `/api/dashboard/clients` as staff, but both are blocked from `/api/consent/capture`, which admits owner, admin, closer and funding_advisor only; their own intended pages say that block is correct, so this is a real gap between what the screen asks of those two desks and what the system lets them do, and nothing was changed to close it; (5) **the funding chips fail closed on an unrecorded product type**, which is the safe posture `src/config/product-path.mjs` requires, but it means a client whose tier has never been written sees no funding chip at all — that is honest rather than wrong, and it will look like the funding chips are missing until `clients.outcome_tier` is filled in; (6) **these generated pages cannot show a screen change at all** — they record routes and gates, so a reader comparing intended against actual will see nothing of this work on either page, and this changelog line is the only record of it in `docs/journeys/`; (7) Phase 0's twelve live bugs are untouched and remain Chris's for a later batch — in particular `crs_count` on the client list still counts demo rows as real pulls, and the workflow at `src/workflows/c-03-inquiry-removed-resume-or-hold.mjs:45` still writes "Apply for Funding" onto a repair-only client's record without checking what they bought; The same migration creates `agent_triggers` and `agent_runs`, the two tables the screen's '0 triggers' and '0 runs' counters have never had a source for, both with their RLS shape declared (ENABLE, FORCE, named policy) so they cannot repeat the six-dispute-tables failure T16 fixed. `src/agents/runtime.mjs` now writes an `agent_runs` row at one boundary point on every wake, including the wakes that decided to do nothing — that outcome is by far the most common and was recorded nowhere. **BEHAVIOUR CHANGE worth its own line:** the Agent Editor's Save button now records WHERE an agent runs (`runtime`, `runtime_ref`), appended to the end of the UPDATE because `src/http/agents-write.test.mjs` reads those parameters positionally; until now a saved agent could never be pointed at a real call. Now an owner or admin can approve or refuse a held post from the waiting list on Social Studio, and approve or send back a submitted brand from the Creative Factory review queue. **APPROVE AND REFUSE ARE OWNER/ADMIN ONLY, enforced on the server, not by hiding a button** — a partner approving their own credit-repair post would defeat the entire hold. That tightening is the only journey delta: `/api/social/posts` moves from `partner, staff` to `employees: owner, admin` plus partner, matching the shape `/api/brand/review` already has. **Recorded fidelity loss:** the extractor reads a gate per endpoint, so the page now describes the whole of `/api/social/posts` as owner/admin-limited when in truth only approve and refuse are — list, queue and discard still serve a partner. Three defects found by adversarial review and fixed before merge, each PROVEN by a test that fails against the pre-fix code: (1) a refusal did not check whether it had actually changed a row, so a post moved by a concurrent approve could be reported refused while it stayed queued and still sent — the refuse arm now RETURNs and throws the same NOT_HELD the approve arm already threw, and because both statements share one transaction the mirror-row write rolls back with it; (2) `withPartnerScope({kind:'partner'})` scopes row-level security to the NAMED partner, not to the caller's company, so one company's owner could approve another company's held post — an org predicate copied verbatim from `api/brand/review.mjs` now guards it, applied ONLY to approve and refuse, and a post belonging to another company answers identically to one that does not exist so the endpoint cannot confirm it is there; (3) two screens still told the reader the opposite of what the code does — the approval panel said "nothing can approve one yet", and the copy-check preview a writer reads BEFORE saving said "needing approval does not hold it back… the post still goes out at its time", which had been false since T11 merged. Both rewritten. GAPS recorded, not reconciled: (1) **no `-intended.md` names an approval step**, so this is recorded as a gate change only and no `-intended.md` was edited; (2) **nothing records WHO refused a post** — there is no `rejected_by` column on either table and none was added; approving does record who and when, refusing is distinguishable from an automatic refusal only by the reason written onto the row; (3) **approve and refuse sit behind the existing "is this partner's marketing suite switched on" check**, so switching a partner's marketing off — the natural reaction to a marketing problem — locks their held posts in place permanently; that ordering is an owner decision and was deliberately not changed; (4) posts already handed to a social account had appeared on no tab at all, so they were added to the waiting list — without that an approved post would have vanished and looked deleted, but it means the waiting count rises on first load for posts that were always there. | 2026-08-19 owner direction after the T11 merge: a post the copy check held, and a brand a partner submitted, both sat in a queue that nothing in the product could empty | (this commit)

Five cases are proven in `docs/workflows/fix-2026-08-18/evidence/T14/gate-cases.json`: no key, slot-picked-but-never-filled, week-old record, already-happened call, and a real fresh booking — only the last is claimed as booked. (3) The **application promised a second step that was never built**. `02a-apply-top.html` hard-coded "Application · Step 1 of 2"; grep proves the string "Step 2 of 2" exists nowhere in the repo, and the real shape is contact, then eight ClickFunnels survey cards, then book. The false counter is removed rather than a step invented. (4) The **incomplete-application nudge chased people who had finished**. Its completion check read `events` by `client_id`, but `api/public/survey-submit.mjs` emitted `entry.captured` and `survey.submitted` with no client at all, so the check could never succeed and the "they completed it" branch was dead code. Both halves are fixed: the endpoint now resolves-or-creates the client and stamps both events with the id, and the check also matches the email inside the payload so rows written before today still resolve. Note the job itself was already switched back on in `f3fb9a7`; the audit finding that it was deregistered is stale. Honesty pass on the education pages: the claims that a student portal exists, that login credentials are issued, that lessons are available immediately, and a decorative progress bar reading 40% complete, are all reworded or removed — none of `courses`, `lessons`, `modules` or `enrollments` has ever existed in `db/migrations`, and `public/education/learn/` is a plain page saying so rather than a faked player. Fixed inside the screen; `shell.js` is T0's and was not touched. **Creative enqueue was rejected by the database for an owner:** `generation_jobs.requested_by` is a foreign key to `accounts(id)` and the handler wrote a staff id, which are separate tables that can never coincide, so the insert raised and the handler answered 500. `db/migrations/241` supersedes 045 with the typed-requester shape 077 already uses (kind + one FK per kind); the old column is kept, nullable and never written again, because NULL means unknown and must survive. **BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, the biggest in this thread:** `db/migrations/240` adds `awaiting_approval` to `social_posts` and redefines 049's screen-gate trigger so a post the compliance engine marks `needs_approval` can only reach the holding pen, never `queued`. Before this, `src/social/scheduler.mjs` treated `needs_approval` and `passed` identically and 049's own trigger allowed it, so a post the engine said needed a person went out anyway. `src/compliance/screen.mjs` returns `needs_approval` for `credit_repair` **unconditionally** — its comment says the gate cannot be turned off — and **nothing anywhere writes `social_posts.approved_at`**, so from this branch forward every credit-repair social post stops and stays stopped. That is the safe direction and it is deliberate, but it is a stop, and it is recorded here as one. 240 also moves already-queued posts into the pen, but ONLY those whose screening said `needs_approval` and never `passed`; a `passed` post is left where it is, a `posting` post is left alone, and a queued post with no screening row at all is reported rather than guessed at. Proven by seeding a real identity, invoking the handler and scanning every value in the response.
