2026-08-18 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | Bank-application launcher, proxy and lenders, T8 of the 2026-08-18 fix batch. **Every `-actual.md` regenerated byte-identical and that is the correct result:** no route was added or removed and no role gate moved — all eight T8 handlers were already in the ROUTES map, verified one by one, and `api/proxy/launch.mjs` keeps the `requireAuth` + separate `requireRole` shape the extractor recognises. Three real defects fixed, each proven broken on https://fundhub.ai on 2026-08-18 before it was touched. (1) Pressing Apply on a bank application answered 503 and recorded **nothing** — `src/proxy/launch.mjs` threw on missing Oxylabs credentials BEFORE `insertProxySession`, so `proxy_sessions` stayed at 0 rows and the attempt was invisible to the audit trail. The check is gone; `launchCredentials()` already returns the same `oxylabs_credentials_missing` error and the same 503 further down, AFTER the pending row exists, so a refused launch now leaves a row at `status='failed'` with `error_code` set. Behaviour change worth its own line: a client with no city or state on file now reports `client_location_missing` instead of the credentials error, because that check now runs first — that is the more useful of the two answers and it is deliberate. (2) The failure box on the Apply screen showed an error code and nothing else, so the manual fallback the audit went looking for never appeared. It now carries a plain `next_step` per failure and states that browser routing is **NOT** active and the application was **not** opened. It deliberately does **not** print host, port, username or password on a failure: the session never came up, nothing was geo-verified, and printing settings that were never checked would be a lie about where the bank thinks you are. (3) `src/banking/accounts-store.mjs` built `ON CONFLICT` without repeating either partial index's `WHERE` predicate (081 line 123, 103 line 162), so Postgres found no arbiter and every provider sync died 42P10 on its very first account — no bank account has ever reached `bank_accounts` through that path against a real database. Fake-database tests could not see it. Also fixed: `api/lenders.mjs` could answer `ok:true` with `lender:null`, which the Lenders screen wrote straight back into its row cache, costing the row its id and breaking that row's next Save; it now returns 409 `save_failed`. GAPS recorded, not reconciled: (1) **no `-intended.md` file describes screen behaviour at all** — they record which role can open which route and nothing more — so nothing hand-written covers the Apply failure copy, the Lenders import, or a connect-a-bank control, and the wording decisions above are recorded here and in code comments instead; (2) `read/proxy-sessions` is routed and gated {owner, funding_advisor} but **no page in `public/` fetches it**, so even a successful proxy session is invisible on screen — T8 did not add a page, because that is a new CRM surface and the owner's call; (3) real bank linking stays an open, honest seam — `src/banking/plaid.mjs` refuses with `not_configured` / `not_implemented` and never returns an empty account list as if it were the truth, and its own header says an agent must not close it; (4) the `lenders` table is still empty for the org, so lender matching honestly returns 0 — the import path is now proven end to end against a scratch Postgres with obviously-fake fixture rows, but no real lender was invented to make the count non-zero. | 2026-08-18 live audit: pressing launch on the bank-application tool failed with a server error and left no record it had ever been tried, and no client could ever match a lender | (this commit)

The generator classes it exactly where it belongs — with `/api/contracts/sign` and `/api/soft-pull-approve` under "genuinely open, no sign-in needed, reachable by anyone and not by this journey in particular" — so every `-actual.md` moved from 174 routes to 175 and from 15 open routes to 16, and NO role's can-reach or blocked list changed. That is the correct result: this is a page a person opens while signed OUT, and the signed token in the URL is the credential. Also changed, and invisible to these pages because they track routes and gates rather than behaviour: an inbound email whose whole body is STOP now records an opt-out on the email channel; Mailgun's own `unsubscribed` event does the same instead of being ignored; and an inbound text from a client whose stored phone is not in +1 format now resolves to that client instead of nobody. GAPS recorded, not reconciled: (1) no `-intended.md` names an unsubscribe step, because none of them describes any public no-session page — they describe API routes reachable by a signed-in principal, so `/unsubscribe.html` is outside what they cover, the same way `/contract.html` and `/portal-login.html` already are; (2) the route counts in every `-intended.md` are still the 2026-08-02 numbers and now trail actual by more than this change alone; (3) no `-intended.md` describes opt-out or consent-withdrawal behaviour on any channel, so nothing hand-written covers whether an emailed STOP should stop email — that judgment is recorded here and in the code comments instead. | 2026-08-18 live audit: a client emailed STOP, it was filed as an ordinary message, `opt_outs` held zero rows, and 173 email templates promised an unsubscribe link that did not exist and pointed at a page that 404'd | (this commit)
2026-08-18 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | Database and security, T16 of the 2026-08-18 fix batch. Three things changed and none of them is a route or a role gate. (1) Six credit-dispute tables were locked shut on the live database — the row lock was switched on outside the migration files and no key was ever attached, which in Postgres means deny everything to the ordinary app login. The app read zero rows and could not write at all, and neither failure raised an error, so the whole credit-dispute feature has been quietly doing nothing. `db/migrations/200_dispute_rls_policies.sql` unlocks all six and, importantly, DECLARES the lock state in a file so a test database and the live database finally describe the same thing — the existing guard could never see this because the drift did not live in the files it reads. `201_no_bare_rls_sweep.sql` is the standing net for the next table that drifts the same way; this is the third time this class of bug has shipped (109, 154, now 160/161). (2) Deleting a demo client failed with a server error and left the client behind while destroying its own data first. The delete list was hand-written, named 3 of the 16 tables that can refuse, and silenced every error but the last. It now reads the list from the database itself, runs as one atomic statement, and reports which table refused. (3) `202_client_fk_indexes.sql` indexed 23 columns that point at a client and had no index, which is what made that delete slow enough to time out. **Every `-actual.md` regenerated byte-identical, and that is the correct result:** no route was added or removed, no role gate moved, and `api/demo/simulate.mjs` keeps the exact `requireAuth` + separate `requireRole` shape the journey extractor recognises — deliberately, because two other routes already sit in the extractor's UNVERIFIED list and this one must not join them. Journeys track routes and gates, not database row locks. GAPS recorded, not reconciled: (1) no `-intended.md` file describes the database, row locks, or demo teardown at all, so nothing hand-written covers any of this work — the audit that found it used a board order, not a journey; (2) 147 of 175 tables carry an allow-all row policy, so one shared app login can read every client's file — that is an owner-level decision about the product's security model, deliberately NOT changed here, and it is recorded on the fix board; (3) `affiliate_payout_lines` carries a guard trigger with no demo exemption, so a demo client whose commission landed in a paid affiliate run still cannot be torn down — it now fails loudly by name instead of silently. | 2026-08-18 live audit: the credit-dispute feature had nowhere to read or write, and pressing delete on a demo client errored and left it there | (this commit)
2026-08-18 | (no journey changed by this work — recorded deliberately) | **COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED** — Background jobs and automations, T6 of the 2026-08-18 fix batch. Two workflows that existed on disk but were never served are now served — `s-02-incomplete-survey-nudge` (on `entry.captured`, 400 rows and nothing listening) and `inquiry-call-sweeper` (cron `*/15`) — an **owner decision taken 2026-08-19**; `src/workflows/index.test.mjs` now fails if any `inngest.createFunction` on disk is neither served nor named in `DELIBERATELY_UNSERVED` with a reason, so 53-written/51-served cannot hide again. `GET /api/read/workflows` stopped reporting `engine_active` from a single env-var check and stopped calling a workflow "live" because an event row shares its name; statuses are now `scheduled` / `trigger_seen` / `trigger_never_seen` / `no_emitter`, each row carries `can_start_today` with a plain reason naming the switched-off secret, and `run_records_available` is always false because nothing in this database records a workflow finishing. `POST /api/journeys/run` now excludes demo journey rows (they have no start event, fired nothing, and their presence stopped the six real journeys being walked at all) and reports the skipped keys; the runner passes `skipInngest: true` so the always-rolled-back harness can no longer start real automations over the network; `PUT /api/journeys` validates node config per step type. **Every `-actual.md` regenerated byte-identical, and that is the correct result:** no route was added or removed and no role gate moved — `journeys`, `journeys/run` and `read/workflows` keep the gates they had (`FINANCE` after `requireAuth`, `STAFF` for the registry read). These pages track routes and gates, not workflow registration or screen wording. GAPS recorded, not reconciled: (1) no `-intended.md` names the Automations or Journeys screen behaviour at all — they describe route reach only — so nothing hand-written covers what either screen should say, which is why the wording changes were made against the audit evidence rather than against an intended journey; (2) `role-owner-intended.md` does not say who may run a journey test, so the existing owner/admin gate was left exactly as found; (3) the six journey keys the runner knows (`client`, `setter`, `closer`, `advisor`, `affiliate`, `partner`) still do not match the eight journeys these pages track — `role-owner` and `role-inquiry-remover` have no tree, recorded here and in the runner's own header, not invented. | 2026-08-18 live audit: the Automations screen told the owner 49 of 51 jobs were live when the count had risen only because auditors fired test events, two clock jobs were labelled dead while working, and the journey test reported "0 of 51 automations reached" while quietly able to fire live automations | (this commit)

2026-08-02 | client, role-owner, role-sales-manager, role-closer, role-funding-advisor, role-inquiry-remover, affiliate, white-label | One route added — `POST /api/documents-upload` (staff + client), client file uploads end to end. Adds a fifth `documents.kind` (`client_upload`, db/migrations/118_client_uploads.sql — renumbered from 117 during merge, main already had 117_account_magic_links.sql) so uploaded files go through the existing documents registry — same versioning, same signed-download path — instead of a second system. Files are sniffed by magic number (jpg/png/pdf only, 10 MB default cap) and stored via a new Netlify Blobs provider in src/documents/store.mjs, the storage decision for this build. A successful upload emits the existing canonical `docs.received` event (not a new `docs.uploaded` — none exists anywhere in this codebase; `docs.received` is already what src/workflows/f-06-funding-conditions-missing-docs.mjs listens for to clear a client's "Missing Documents" hold). The signed-download route (`/api/documents/:id`) now streams bytes through the store for opaque, non-URL storage keys instead of only redirecting — Netlify Blobs keys aren't public URLs the way Vercel Blob's were, so the redirect-only path could not have served them. Route lives at `/api/documents-upload`, not `/api/documents/upload`, because src/http/routes.test.mjs forbids an exact ROUTES key that would sit under the `documents/` prefix branch (the signed-download route) and depend on lookup order. Full non-pg suite green before commit; DATABASE_URL unset in this sandbox so the new .pg.test.mjs (upload, reject invalid type/size, storage round-trip, event emission) could not run here — see docs/UPLOADS-SPEC.md for what needs verifying against a real database | (this commit)

2026-08-02 | client, role-owner, role-sales-manager, role-closer, role-funding-advisor, role-inquiry-remover, affiliate, white-label | Launch-readiness pass on the staff reply inbox. No route added or removed, so the totals hold at 80 — but three gates changed behaviour. (1) OWNERS NO LONGER CLOCK IN: `requireActiveShift` gained an opt-in `exempt` option and all three shift-gated endpoints (api/messages, api/inquiries, api/tasks) pass SUPER_ROLES, closing the question ACTIVE-SHIFT-ROLLOUT.md §3c and §4 explicitly refused to guess. An exempt caller's work is recorded against a NULL shift, which is the honest value rather than an invented one. (2) HELD MESSAGES NOW ACTUALLY SEND: netlify/functions/staff-message-sweeper.mjs, a Netlify scheduled function every five minutes, scoped to messages a person wrote. (3) The texting-hours rule is stated on the compose box before anything is typed | Owner decisions, 2026-08-02, plus two defects found while acting on them. The overnight hold was enforced and announced nowhere, so the first anyone knew of it was a reply that did not go — and the screen's promise that a held text 'will go out on its own' was untrue, because nothing in this repository had ever called the dispatcher on a clock. The sweeper is deliberately NOT the Inngest one: registering that needs INNGEST_EVENT_KEY, which CLAUDE.md §11 reserves for the owner because it makes 47 workflows live, and it would drain thousands of stale workflow-queued messages onto real people. A migration repointing SMS to Twilio was written on the owner's stated direction and then WITHDRAWN — acting on it surfaced A2P 10DLC, which three places in this repo already record as the reason Twilio ships disabled; unregistered carrier traffic is filtered silently, and a migration runs unattended. 118 is a documented no-op carrying the switch for a human. The related real defect is unfixed and named: the GHL relay addresses by ghl_contact_id, which is NULL for every client created since the cutover, so those customers cannot receive a text reply. Also: the inbox list query was rebuilt so it stops scanning the whole conversations table per page load, the needs-reply tab moved server-side, and Playwright landed — CLAUDE.md §6 gate 4, listed as required since the file was written and never once runnable. 18 browser tests, blocking in CI. Full spec in docs/REPLY-INBOX-SPEC.md | (this commit)
2026-08-02 | client, role-owner, role-sales-manager, role-closer, role-funding-advisor, role-inquiry-remover, affiliate, white-label | Three routes added — `GET /api/read/inbox` and `GET /api/read/messages` (both STAFF-gated) and `POST /api/messages` (staff principals only, and gated on an open shift). Route total moves to 80 everywhere. The staff reply inbox is built end to end: an inbound SMS or a client's own email now finds-or-creates that client's conversation for that channel and threads the message onto it; messaging.html stops being sample markup and becomes a real inbox (conversation list newest-first with a needs-reply marker, thread view, compose box, client context panel), and a staff reply goes out through the existing dispatcher and the existing compliance gate. `messages.sender_staff_id` (120, renumbered from 117 during merge — main already had 117/118/119) records who wrote it. Inbound email threading is keyed on the sender matching a client record — a bank statement forwarded to a client's monitoring inbox is NOT threaded as though the client had written it | COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED: this is the first path in the repository where an employee types free-form text and it reaches a consumer. Nothing about the gate changed — opt-out, quiet hours and the restricted-wording rules apply to a typed reply exactly as they apply to a template, and compose deliberately does not call the gate itself so there is one verdict taken at send time. Template approval does not apply because there is no template row; that is stated and tested rather than assumed. Two gaps are recorded rather than filled: an emailed reply files no `staff_events` row (the frozen telemetry vocabulary has no kind for an email, and a call site does not get to mint one), and nothing schedules `dispatchDue()`, so a text deferred for quiet hours has nothing to release it at 11am. Full spec in docs/REPLY-INBOX-SPEC.md | (this commit)
2026-08-02 | all eight | No route or screen change — the fix is underneath. Now that the outbound queue actually drains, two things that were safe only because nothing sent are not safe any more, and both are closed. (1) A journey run mints fake clients and puts real-looking messages in the queue; the run-level fence refuses a run when the company's routing can reach the outside world, but the QUEUE OUTLIVES THE ROUTING — run against the memory provider today, connect Mailgun next week, and the next drain mails invented people. The dispatcher now refuses a synthetic client outright whenever the resolved provider can transmit, permanently and with no override. (2) `messaging_settings.alert_email` was collected and never read; three or more failures in one pass now file a task for the owner saying emails are not going out, once per company per day | The fence's own header recorded this as an open question left for whoever plugged sending in — that was the previous commit, so it is answered here rather than left as a note. The rule is the INVERSE of the old one: not "only test clients may be sent to", which refused every real client and is why the fence was unwired in the first place, but "a test client may never be sent to for real." The alert is a task and NOT an email on purpose — it fires exactly when sending is broken, so an emailed alert is the one message guaranteed not to arrive. Also fixed the same committed-DDL race in the outbox test cleanup that was fixed in the hiring tests | (this commit)
2026-08-02 | all eight | The platform can send email at all. One route added — `POST /api/messages-outbound` (`status` is STAFF; `dispatch`, `settings`, `email_invoice` and `email_invoice_backlog` are owner/admin), so route totals and role reach move by one everywhere. Routed as `messages-outbound` rather than `messages` because the already-merged staff-reply-inbox branch claims `POST /api/messages` for a staff reply — both branches independently wrote a handler at `api/messages.mjs` for two unrelated features, and this one was renamed at merge time (see docs/MERGE-LOG.md section 3). One workflow joined the registry (`message-dispatch-sweeper`, 48 -> 49). The change underneath every journey page is that the outbound queue finally drains: twenty-six workflows have been calling `sendTemplated()` and writing queued `messages` rows since they were written, and NOTHING HAS EVER SENT ONE. Invoices were worse — `invoices.status` has included 'sent' since 017 and no code ever emailed one, so two workflows have been raising invoices nobody was told about. `invoices.emailed_at` now records what actually left, separately from what we claimed | The owner asked for invoices by email and for any other sending gaps, and rejected sending as a hidden global switch: "it's not like the workflow is something that we turn on... it should be an option, the ability to set it up... it's a fucking CRM." So the gate moved rather than disappearing — db/migrations/126_outbound_switch.sql gives each company its own `outbound_enabled`, a daily send cap, and a record of who changed it, all visible in the CRM on the ops-admin Outbound Mail panel. INNGEST_EVENT_KEY is still unset and still the owner's to set (CLAUDE.md §11); registering the sweeper sends nothing without it, and the same drain runs today from a button. The compliance gate, the daily cap and the absence of provider credentials are all unchanged. Flagged COMPLIANCE REVIEW REQUIRED — the invoice email is fee-timing copy. Full decision record in docs/CONTRACTS-SPEC.md Part 4 | (this commit)
2026-08-02 | all eight | Contracts now really send, and chase. No new routes: `/api/contracts` gained `run_reminders`, and one workflow joined the registry (`contract-chaser`, 47 -> 48), so every journey page's route totals and role reach are unchanged. What changed underneath them is that the outbound path finally has a CALLER — src/messaging/dispatch.mjs has said in its own header since it was written that "dispatchDue() runs when something calls it, and today nothing does", and that was true of the whole platform, not of contracts in particular. Pressing Send now queues one message per signer carrying that person's own link and hands THOSE ROWS ONLY to the dispatcher | The owner asked why it did not send, and pointed out that chasing is just a workflow. Both fair. Dispatching by id rather than calling dispatchDue() keeps the blast radius to the one contract: draining the whole queue is the switch CLAUDE.md §11 reserves to the owner, and it stays unthrown. The chaser IS registered — which is not the same act, because Inngest invokes nothing until INNGEST_EVENT_KEY is set — and it also runs today without Inngest through the new endpoint and a button on the Contracts screen. The message dispatch sweeper deliberately stays out of the registry and its guard test still passes | (this commit)
2026-08-02 | all eight | The contract generator becomes a signing service. No new routes — the three added earlier the same day gained actions instead (`upload_template`, `save_fields`, `create_client` on `/api/contracts`; file serving and signer lists on `/api/read/contracts`; per-signer links, file fetch and decline on `/api/contracts/sign`), so the route totals and role reach on every journey are unchanged. What moved on these pages is the client-facing surface: `/api/contracts/sign` now serves the DOCUMENT ITSELF to an unauthenticated caller, and remains one of the six genuinely open routes. A PDF can be uploaded, boxes dragged onto its pages, and the whole thing sent to several people who sign IN AN ORDER — under a sequential contract the second signer cannot open the document at all until the first has signed, refused at the endpoint rather than hidden on the screen | The owner asked for DocuSign inside the CRM ("we upload PDFs and do the contract thing... put in fields, like date, name, address, whatever, from the client information") with questions closed, so every remaining decision was taken by the build agent and written into docs/CONTRACTS-SPEC.md Part 2. Two dependencies were added under that grant: pdf-lib to fill and flatten PDFs, and pdf.js vendored into public/ so pages render with no build step and no third-party host. Storage went through src/documents/store.mjs, the same document store every other upload in this codebase already used — an earlier draft of this branch proposed its own competing storage design (a Postgres-backed fallback provider, auto-selected from BLOB_READ_WRITE_TOKEN/DATABASE_URL), which conflicted with the already-merged uploads branch's simpler design and was discarded at merge time rather than kept alongside it (docs/MERGE-LOG.md section 3). Additive throughout: source_kind defaults to 'text', and all 66 tests written for the typed-copy contracts pass unchanged | (this commit)
2026-08-02 | all eight | Three routes added — `POST /api/contracts` (staff write surface: template authoring, drafts, preview, send, void), `GET /api/read/contracts` (the library, the queue, and one contract with its frozen wording) and `GET/POST /api/contracts/sign`. The third is the important one on these pages: it is the second route in the whole platform that takes NO SESSION AT ALL, so it joins `/api/documents/<id>` in the "genuinely open" list on every journey and the open-route count moves from 5 to 6. Auth on it is the HMAC in the link, not a principal — which is why it reads as open here and is not. One new CRM screen (contracts.html, in every sidebar, `ROLE_SETS.STAFF` with an owner/admin-only wording card inside it) and one client page outside the CRM (`/contract.html`, no shell.js, no session) | The contract generator: a staff member writes a contract once, sends it to any client, and gets it back signed, with no developer involved at any step. Built against 030's documents/document_versions rather than a new immutability scheme — the rendered wording is frozen at send and hash-checked at signature against `document_versions.checksum`, so a contract altered after sending cannot be signed at all. Regenerated with `npm run journeys` from the code, per CLAUDE.md §4, not hand-edited. NOTE THE GAP THIS DOES NOT CLOSE: there is no `*-intended.md` anywhere in this directory, so nothing was checked against an intended journey before building — reported rather than reconciled. Full decision record in docs/CONTRACTS-SPEC.md | (this commit)
